| B11-mun-orbit | nightly | COMMIT-IN-FOREIGN-SOI (the new D1 cell): a recording that ENDS parked in another body's SOI and is COMMITTED there - the commit path, the terminal classification, and the background-recording handoff for a tree whose terminal state is "in orbit around the Mun". Every other lunar/interplanetary case (B5/B6/B7) flies THROUGH an SOI and comes back or continues, so none of them reaches this surface. Machine: the LIVE-PROVEN `mlib.b5_decide` with the new `captureEnabled` param - ascent, transfer, TLI, corrections, warp policy all byte-identical to the 26 B5 flights; NEW is the four-phase tail PLAN-CAPTURE (MechJeb circularize-at-periapsis) -> CAPTURE-BURN (NodeExecutor, autowarp EXPLICIT; done evidence is a BOUND orbit, since a hyperbolic approach reads a NEGATIVE apoapsis) -> PARK (throttle cut, nodes cleared, SAS+RCS held, rails dropped to 1x, 180 game-s held dwell) -> ORBIT-COMMIT (the B-DOCK route-1 mid-mission seam CommitTree) -> ORBIT-COMMITTED. Leaving the target SOI anywhere in that tail is an ASSERT-FAIL, so B5's free-return cannot green this mission | D1 auto-record-launch + commit-in-foreign-soi (the NEW cell); D3 orbital-checkpoint; D4 atmospheric / exo-propulsive / exo-ballistic / cohesive-cross-body-coast; D14 kerbin/mun/warp-rails (D5 bg-recording was CLAIMED and then REMOVED 2026-07-25: `CommitTreeFlight` nulls both `backgroundRecorder` and `PhysicsFramePatch.BackgroundRecorderInstance` before returning, no assertion or token covers a handoff, and `settle_frames = 0` ends the mission on the commit frame - BDOCK-1 is the honest claimant of that cell). LIVE-PROVEN 2026-07-25 (flight 2 FULL PASS attempt 1, all seven verifiers green, wall 1,268 s, analyzer red=0): capture apoapsis flipped -1,560,099 -> +138,789 m at eccentricity 0.000127, all six assertions met, PARK -> ORBIT-COMMIT -> ORBIT-COMMITTED. Its coast issued the native warp ONCE with ZERO cancels, which is why the Mun lane never showed B12 flight 2's coast warp-thrash; the shared fix for that landed after this pass and does not alter any frame this flight took (a coast that never cancels a valid warp is unaffected). CONFIRMATION RE-FLY PAID (flight 3, 2026-07-25): B12 flight 3's periapsis-bound fix CHANGED this mission's flown profile - TARGET-FLYBY now warps to periapsis_ut - 900 instead of riding the rails flyby stair, and the COAST handoff stops the inherited warp - so a LIVE-PROVEN mission owed one flight on the changed profile. It flew FULL PASS again: wall 1,269 s, all six assertions met, capture eccentricity 0.000127 (flight 2 read the same number, so the profile change did not move the capture quality), and TARGET-FLYBY collapsed from 8,213 game seconds to 27 game seconds on 2 warp commands. The lane's re-fly debt is now clear on both axes. FLIGHT 4 (2026-07-25) FULL PASS, wall 1,271 s (`results/2026-07-25_0400_B11-mun-orbit.json`): the COUNT-PIN run - the first B11 pass carrying `verifiers.expectations.observed.recordings.count`, which read 8 and pinned the window to {8, 8}. **FLIGHT 5 (2026-07-25) FULL PASS attempt 1, wall 1,270.195 s - the POST-REVIEW re-fly, and the first flight on which this mission's headline claim is actually VERIFIED.** Owed because the review pass added a `time_to_periapsis > 0` conjunct to the capture arming gate (a frame-level change to a path the green flights took) and because the new commit-terminal token needed its first live proof. Both landed: the token reads `terminalState=Orbiting terminalOrbitBody=Mun` for the parked craft, and the 8-recording topology is now legible instead of merely counted - 1 `Orbiting Mun` (the committed craft), 6 `Destroyed` (the radial boosters), 1 `Orbiting Kerbin` (the flameout-staged ascent core), matching the count-pin derivation exactly. GATED since 2026-07-26 (review round 2): a regression that drops one recording while adding a spurious one still reads 8, so the count alone cannot catch it - and until this round only the Mun row was actually required. BOTH SPECS WERE THEN GIVEN `terminalState=Orbiting terminalOrbitBody=Kerbin` and `terminalState=Destroyed terminalOrbitBody=(null)` so each of the three topology CLASSES was asserted (the count still owns the total) - measured on the same fixture and launcher in `logs/2026-07-25_1216_B7-duna-flyby/KSP.log`: 8 terminal lines, 1 `Orbiting Kerbin` and 6 `Destroyed (null)`. READ THAT IN THE PAST TENSE: at HEAD only B12 still REQUIRES the Kerbin row; B11 carries it as a FORBIDDEN token instead (next sentence). **THAT KERBIN ROW WAS WRONG FOR THIS LANE AND IS REMOVED (2026-08-08).** It was transplanted from a B7 flight - a different profile sharing only the fixture and launcher - and B11 did not fly again until 2026-08-08, so its first live outing on B11 red it twice (runs `2026-08-08_1355` wall 1320 s and `_1419` wall 1318 s, both MISSION-OK with every other verifier green and count 8). On the Mun profile the flameout watchdog fires TWICE during CORRECTION-BURN at alt ~3.60 Mm with `thr=0.250 avThr=0.000`, and the EVENT TYPES must be named because the two are ~0.5 s apart: the DEBOUNCE ONSETS (`gate flameoutStreak 0->1`) are ut 4899.812 / 4901.392 and the STAGE FIRES (`gate flameoutStages 0->1`, `1->2`) are ut 4900.312 / 4901.912, quoting run `_1419`; all three flights agree on all four to within 0.2 s (an earlier draft of this row paired one onset with one fire). The remnant leaves on a Kerbin-IMPACTING trajectory and reads `Destroyed` on ALL THREE flights - its recording start matches the `flameoutStages 0->1` fire to the millisecond (4900.4185835266071 / 4900.3124958801336 / 4900.475848693869). THE ROW THAT ACTUALLY FLIPS `Destroyed` vs `Landed Kerbin` is one of the FIRST ascent-shed radial-booster pair (recording start ~48.46/48.48, down beside the pad: in the committed fixture `998cc41e` Landed and its same-UT twin `a834e40a` Destroyed, while `_1355` had both Destroyed) - the one genuinely unstable facet, now documented in the spec header instead of required. Two earlier drafts pinned that variance on the remnant and one of them wrote "Destroyed (7x on _1355)", conflating a whole-run tally with a single row; correcting the attribution makes the re-pin BETTER supported, because the remnant is the STABLE row and it never once ends in Kerbin orbit. Root-caused before re-pinning: `_b5_flameout_stage` byte-identical to the last-green baseline `aedc47092`, flameout / `CORR*` / throttle constants unchanged, `pins.toml` and the `b2-lko-craft` fixture untouched, and **B12 PASSED the same day with all three tokens** - so no shared decision path regressed and the Kerbin remnant is a Minmus-profile property (full forensics: `B11-TERMINAL-TOKEN-NEVER-TRUE` in todo-and-known-bugs.md). **RE-PINNED AND RE-FLOWN GREEN 2026-08-08: run `2026-08-08_1458` PASS attempt 1, wall 1321 s / mission 1270 s, mismatches=[], count 8**, and that run's produced save is the committed **`mun-orbit-recorded`** recorded-state fixture for the moon loop-validation lanes (harvested `--keep-parsek` through the `--expect-situation ORBITING` gate; 56 sidecars, 8 recordings, terminals `{Orbiting 1, Landed 1, Destroyed 6}`, branchPoints `{JointBreak 5}`, parked craft SMA 339,568.968 m / ecc 0.000126 / ref Mun = ~139.6 km altitude; pinned by the `RECORDED_FIXTURES` sweep in `harness/lib/test_saveparse.py`). Flight 1 (2026-07-24) flaked at CAPTURE-BURN on our own no-start watchdog colliding with MechJeb's 600 s pre-ignition WARPALIGN hold; fixed with an OBSERVED NodeExecutor.Enabled channel + a node-clock no-start classifier (forensics in todo-and-known-bugs.md) |
| B12-minmus-orbit | nightly | Same cells on the minmus axis (a thin alias over the same capture-enabled machine, exactly as B6 is to B5) | D1 auto-record-launch + commit-in-foreign-soi; D3 orbital-checkpoint; D4 atmospheric / exo-propulsive / exo-ballistic / cohesive-cross-body-coast; D14 kerbin/minmus/warp-rails (D5 bg-recording removed 2026-07-25 for the same reason as B11 - nothing gates it). LIVE-PROVEN 2026-07-25 (flight 4 FULL PASS, all six mission assertions met, wall 580 s; flight 5 repeated it at wall 580 s and was the COUNT-PIN run - the first B12 pass carrying `verifiers.expectations.observed.recordings.count`, which read 8 and pinned the window to {8, 8}, `results/2026-07-25_0349_B12-minmus-orbit.json`; **FLIGHT 6 FULL PASS attempt 1, wall 580.826 s - the POST-REVIEW re-fly that first VERIFIES the lane's headline claim**: the new commit-terminal token reads `terminalState=Orbiting terminalOrbitBody=Minmus` for the parked craft, and the 8-recording topology is now legible instead of merely counted - 1 `Orbiting Minmus`, 6 `Destroyed` boosters, 1 `Orbiting Kerbin` core, matching the count-pin derivation exactly. GATED since 2026-07-26 (review round 2): the spec required only the target-body row, so a regression dropping the ascent-core recording and adding a spurious booster would still read 8 and still pass - both specs were then given `terminalState=Orbiting terminalOrbitBody=Kerbin` and `terminalState=Destroyed terminalOrbitBody=(null)`, measured on the same fixture and launcher in `logs/2026-07-25_1216_B7-duna-flyby/KSP.log` (1 and 6 lines). AT HEAD THAT IS TRUE OF B12 ONLY: B11 removed the Kerbin row on 2026-08-08 (it was never true of the Mun profile) and now FORBIDS it, so do not read "both specs" as current - B12 keeps the row because its own 2026-08-08 flight measured it. Owed because the review added a `time_to_periapsis > 0` arming conjunct that changes a frame the green flights took. NOTE the flight-6 lesson: its FIRST attempt red'd PARSEK-FAIL on the new token and looked exactly like "the commit records the wrong terminal body", but the flight had run the PREVIOUS DLL - harness flights use the provisioned `automation/stock-minimal` instance, and `dotnet build` only deploys to the dev instance. Run `provision.py --profile stock-minimal` after any C# change; see the note in `.claude/CLAUDE.md`): capture eccentricity 0.00026, PARK -> ORBIT-COMMIT -> ORBIT-COMMITTED, and the two shared-machine warp fixes this mission's own forensics produced both held - COAST-TO-TARGET flew 194,543 game seconds in 26 wall seconds (ratio 7,535) on 3 warp commands, and the periapsis-bounded TARGET-FLYBY armed the capture on the orbit's clock instead of blowing through it. At 580 s wall it is the CHEAPEST of the two orbit cases (B11 costs 1,269 s), which makes the Minmus axis the better default for a fast regression check of the shared capture machine. PRIOR FLIGHTS (the forensics that produced three of the lane's four findings): FLIGHT 3 FLOWN 2026-07-25: the coast fix WORKED (COAST-TO-TARGET 26 wall / 194,704 game = ratio 7,543 on 3 warp commands, down from never-finishing) and the run reached a capture burn. THIRD SHARED-machine defect found, named at a glance by the new warpUtilisation block: TARGET-FLYBY read 2 wall / 8,213 game (ratio 5,341, 2 commands) and blew straight through periapsis - entered at ut 268,934.5 / alt 1,902 km descending at -236 m/s, PLAN-CAPTURE at ut 277,147.5 / alt 41,609 m CLIMBING at +92 m/s. Two compounding causes: the COAST -> TARGET-FLYBY handoff emitted NO warp cleanup so the craft crossed the SOI still running the coast's RAILSx10000 warp (the first flyby poll alone advanced 3,907 game seconds), and capture mode fell through to a rails flyby stair floored at flybyWarpFactor whose distance term knows nothing about the periapsis CLOCK. The late burn produced a bound but wildly eccentric 325 x 5.3 km orbit grazing Minmus, CORRECTLY rejected by the capture window as an under-burn. FIXED: `mlib.capture_flyby_warp_target` - the only legitimate warp target inside the target SOI is `periapsis_ut - CAPTURE_PERIAPSIS_WARP_LEAD_SECONDS` (900, covering our arming + plan, MechJeb's halfBurnTime ignition lead and its 600 s pre-ignition hold), read from `Orbit.TimeToPeriapsis` via the opt-in `read_periapsis`; past the bound or with the clock unreadable the machine does NOT warp (fail closed, 1x); and the handoff now stops the inherited coast warp. FLIGHT 2: the correction fix WORKED (both rounds cleared, `rounds=2`, round 1's 73,720 game-second aim-warp ran as ONE continuous warp from ut 475.3 to 74,195.7 with no cancel), then INVALID `mission-budget-expired` inside COAST-TO-TARGET at ut 225,990 with 41,655 game seconds still to go. SECOND SHARED-machine defect found: the coast derives its native warp target from `time_to_soi` EVERY poll, and KSP cannot read the patched-conic SOI time while re-patching under a warp ramp - measured over flight 2's COAST frames, tts was finite on 2,451 of 2,451 unwarped frames and NaN on 1,154 of 1,161 warping ones - so the machine cancelled its own warp on the blind read and re-armed on the next unwarped poll: 3,603 `warp_to_ut` issues, 3,602 `cancel_warp`, a rails rate that never escaped ~2.7x, ~40 game-s per wall-s. METASTABLE, which is why it had never been seen: B11 flight 2's Mun coast issued the warp ONCE (0 cancels, 30/30 warping frames finite) and locked in at RAILSx1000. FIXED: `mlib.coast_native_warp_hold` - the target is an ABSOLUTE UT, so a blind read UNDER WARP HOLDS the armed command; only a blind read with the game NOT warping is evidence the encounter is gone. Plus a NAMED `coast-warp-thrash` fast-fail past `MAX_PHASE_WARP_ISSUES` (500; a healthy coast issues 1) and a per-phase `warpUtilisation` block in the mission result whose `gameSecondsPerWallSecond` names this class in one line. Budget RE-DERIVED bottom-up from flight 2's measured spans (~2,280 nominal / ~3,100 worst) and deliberately NOT raised - 4200/4700 stand. FLIGHT 1 (2026-07-25): MISSION-FLAKE at CORRECTION-BURN (wall 286 s), UPSTREAM of the capture tail and in the SHARED B5/B6 machine, not in anything B12-specific. ROOT CAUSE: the no-1x-coast PR (4219832b6) turned the DIY correction burner into AIM-THEN-WARP (aim, then natively warp to `node_ut - nodeArrivalMarginSeconds`, then throttle) but left the phase bounded by `transferBurnTimeoutSeconds` - a GAME-time budget that the warp itself spends. Measured: B11/Mun needs 2,994 s of its 4,000 s budget for that wait (75%, passes), B12/Minmus needs 73,733 s (entry ut 475.3, MechJeb node at ut 74,208.3) and can NEVER pass. FIXED in the shared machine: `mlib.correction_budget_expired` suppresses the budget while an aim-warp is in flight and re-anchors it at the warp ARRIVAL (the same seam that already re-anchors the no-start clock - and a game-time bound cannot bound a STALLED warp anyway, which advances no game time; the runner's warp-stall watchdog + the WALL budget own that), and `mlib.classify_correction_timeout` NAMES the expiry (`correction-burner-no-start` / `correction-burn-incomplete`) so it can never again ride the generic timeout. Every correction round give-up now also carries a `corrGiveup` reason on the machine-diff line. B11 flight 2 passed on this same machine, so no budget number needed changing. Also inherits B11 flight 1's CAPTURE-BURN fix unchanged and `read_node_executor` is on; wall budgets 4200/4700 (raised from 3600/4100 for MechJeb's MEASURED ~600 s pre-ignition hold). Same PROVISIONAL pins; the Minmus-specific one is `captureBurnTimeoutSeconds` 200000 (its SOI edge is ~2,187 km up but arrival speeds are ~5x lower than the Mun's, so the executor's SOI-entry -> periapsis autowarp coast is ~10-20 game hours, not 1-3). **RE-FLOWN GREEN 2026-08-08: run `2026-08-08_1441` PASS attempt 1, wall 630 s, mismatches=[], count 8** - and this is the first flight on which B12's copy of the 2026-07-26 topology tokens is actually LIVE-PROVEN rather than transplanted from the B7 log: it measured 6x `Destroyed (null)` + 1x `Orbiting Kerbin` + 1x `Orbiting Minmus`, so **all three rows hold here and B12 keeps them** (its row was inspected against its own flight rather than assumed to share B11's problem, which it does not - see the B11 row and `B11-TERMINAL-TOKEN-NEVER-TRUE`). That run's produced save is the committed **`minmus-orbit-recorded`** recorded-state fixture for the moon loop-validation lanes (harvested `--keep-parsek` through the `--expect-situation ORBITING` gate; 56 sidecars, 8 recordings, terminals `{Orbiting 2, Destroyed 6}`, branchPoints `{JointBreak 5}`, parked craft SMA 98,463.595 m / ecc 0.000292 / ref Minmus = ~38.5 km altitude; pinned by the `RECORDED_FIXTURES` sweep in `harness/lib/test_saveparse.py`) |

B11/B12 end in ORBIT around a foreign body and B1/B4 land on KERBIN, so nothing else in the suite reaches this end state. Machine: the LIVE-PROVEN `mlib.b5_decide` with the new `landingEnabled` param on top of `captureEnabled` - PRELAUNCH through PARK byte-identical to the five B11 flights; NEW is the tail DESCENT (MechJeb `LandUntargeted`; warp-PASSIVE because MechJeb's landing states own the warp through the shared `Core.Node.Autowarp` flag the runner sets explicitly; `DeployGears` true, `DeployChutes` FALSE because the Mun is airless, `RcsAdjustment` false because the stage has no thruster blocks) -> LANDED-SETTLE (throttle cut, autopilot released, SAS held, rails 1x, a held settled dwell gated on target body + landed situation + BOTH speed components) -> SURFACE-COMMIT (the same route-1 mid-mission seam CommitTree) -> SURFACE-COMMITTED. FOUR named DESCENT give-ups on top of the budget: `landing-autopilot-not-enabled` (COMMANDED-vs-OBSERVED off `LandingAutopilot.Enabled`; a landed frame exits DESCENT BEFORE the supervisor runs, because MechJeb disables its own module on the landed frame and a perfect landing must not read as a dead autopilot), `landing-no-progress` (+ a separate `altitude-unreadable` name), `landing-touchdown-timeout`, `landing-vessel-lost` (a crash reads as neither a timeout nor a success) | LIVE-PROVEN 2026-07-25 FOR THE HAPPY PATH: FULL PASS attempt 1, wall 2,747.9 s, all verifiers green. `terminalState=Landed terminalOrbitBody=Mun`, airless `Approach -> SurfaceStationary`, measured count 8 (window PINNED). Most expensive scenario in the suite at 2,825 s harness wall. NOT live-proven: none of the four DESCENT give-ups nor `landed-never-stable` fired on either flight - they carry unit + fly-loop coverage only (see the COVERAGE HONESTY bullet under roadmap item 3) |

(A 2026-08-01 tag audit briefly ADDED `pending-operator` here on the strength of the spec's PRE-FLIGHT risk note - "no headless test can" verify the inner target - and REVERTED it the same day once this row was read: flight 7 answered that question. The spec now records the trap, and B15 is classified in `REVIEWED_UNTAGGED` as discharged.) MechJeb plans an inner window fine; what it gets wrong is the EJECTION SIZE, because `DeltaVAndTimeForInterplanetaryTransferEjection` computes the post-burn speed at the park's SEMI-MAJOR AXIS and applies it at whatever radius its ejection geometry picks. On a circular park those coincide; on the 0.085-eccentric park MechJeb's own sloppy high-altitude circularization leaves, they do not, and the SAME planner priced the SAME ejection at 652.843 m/s from that park against 775.873 m/s from flight 5's round one, a MEASURED 123.0 m/s shortfall - the heliocentric leg's perihelion sat 2.46e9 m above Eve's aphelion, so no encounter was ever geometrically possible and `nextBody` never once read Eve. mlib still has no direction anywhere; the defect was a THRESHOLD and a MISSING ACTION, not a sign. Fixed by `parkTrimEccMax` (circularize-at-apoapsis before planning) plus a plan-time `reachesTargetOrbit=` verdict. (2) GILLY, and the answer is that it is NOT Ike: 126 km SOI vs 1,050 km (~69x smaller cross-section), 17-57% of the SOI radius vs 6.7%, 12 deg inclination vs 0.2 deg. Residual risk non-zero; a Gilly capture is a NAMED ASSERT-FAIL, deliberately not whitelisted - and it did NOT occur on the green run. GREEN on flight 7 (2026-07-26): full scenario PASS on attempt 1, every verifier PASS/SKIPPED, analyzer red=0, 86 `nextBody=Eve` reads, flyby periapsis 22,032,532 m against the 100,000 m floor, exit to Sun. Both correction rounds flew (378.32 m/s re-aim then a 3.35 m/s trim), which needed `maxCorrectionDvMps` 200 -> 450 - the 200 was a MOON-transfer calibration, and an interplanetary arrival-PHASE fix is legitimately dearer (measured affordable: ~1,944 m/s remained at Sun-SOI entry). That is the all-blind signature of this career fixture's un-upgraded Tracking Station (every telemetry read raises `Maneuver node editing is not available`), and it is the MEASURED proof that CL-1 certifies on the roster channel alone. Gating certification on a craft-channel latch would have red this proven nightly; the latch REPORTS there and CONDEMNS on the terminal. 28 new cells in `test_cl1_crew_loss.py`, three of which reproduce the measured defect with only the conjunct removed |

(`pending-operator` TAG ADDED 2026-08-01: this row already called the promotion "an explicit human call" and described R1's identical state as "the same standard applied to B16 in this same pass" - and R1 carried the tag while B16 did not. A tag audit that inspected only specs MENTIONING `PENDING-OPERATOR` could not see B16, which never writes it; the completeness check now covers operator-tier specs too.) The spec's promotion rule has TWO conditions - fly green AND replace the PROVISIONAL pins with MEASURED ones - and it states the pins need a human reading the result. Only the first condition is met here, so `tier = "operator"` stands and the promotion is left as an explicit human call with the measurements above in hand. RE-FLOWN GREEN 2026-08-11 (`2026-08-11_0718_B16-eve-orbit`, wall 1,843 s / mission 1,769 s, PASS attempt 1, analyzer red=0, 3 report-only Unity NREs in the same stock/third-party teardown classes): that run's produced save is the committed **eve-orbit-recorded** recorded-state fixture for the Eve loop-validation lanes (harvested `--keep-parsek` through the `--expect-situation ORBITING` gate; 56 sidecars, 8 recordings, terminals {Orbiting 2, Destroyed 6} - the Minmus-shaped map, the flameout remnant staying in KERBIN orbit after shedding at the TRANSFER-BURN ejection at ut 11,830,586 - branchPoints {JointBreak 5}, parked craft pe 4,985,446 / ap 4,986,170 m alt, ecc ~0 (`capturedInTargetOrbit` read 0.000), ref Eve; pinned by the RECORDED_FIXTURES sweep in harness/lib/test_saveparse.py). The recordings count window is now PINNED {8, 8} from the two measured flights and the spec's wall prose carries both measurements - the pin half of the promotion rule is closed; the tier decision itself stays the open human call. FIXTURE SPAN, measured off the .prec sidecars: spanStart 34.68, spanEnd 15,764,030.83, span 15,763,996.15 s = 1.0733 Kerbin-Eve synodic periods (14,687,035.42 s) - an Eve loop unit built on this fixture sits in the span>synodic regime (effective cadence != synodic, `PadAlignLaunch` declines), so the consumer lanes must measure the schedule off the `missionconfig applied` echo rather than assume the Duna/moon cadence == synodic shape. |

Measured: pre-tombstone `reservations=1 permanent=1 temporary=0 slots=0`, post-tombstone `reservations=1 permanent=0 temporary=1 slots=1`. That gate would have passed exactly when the tombstone did nothing. Half (ii) is therefore RE-PINNED DEATH-SCOPED in the registry `[D12]` block (explicitly, with the measurement cited, warranted by the registry's own already-death-scoped rationale sentence): after the tombstone the recompute leaves **no PERMANENT, death-derived reservation** - `permanent=0` - while reservations sourced OUTSIDE the supersede write-set legitimately survive as temporary. It is not the relaxation-to-half-(i) the registry note forbids: `permanent=0` is a separate observation of the ELS recompute, falsifiable in the right direction (a tombstone that fails to strip the Dead row leaves `permanent=1` and reds the spec). `CrewReservationManager.RecomputeFromEffectiveLedger` now emits the split (`Recomputed {reason}: N reservations remain (permanent=P temporary=T)`) because the bare count could not discriminate a working strip from a no-op, the spec requires `Recomputed after tombstones: [0-9]+ reservations remain \(permanent=0 `, and **the gate-armed confirm fly `2026-08-04_2324` (PASS attempt 1, 64 s) matched it live** - `Recomputed after tombstones: 1 reservations remain (permanent=0 temporary=1).`, `saveParse status=PASS gating=True armed=['rewind'] mismatches=0` with `supersedeRows=1 tombstones=1`. The R5 verdict is stated at Info TWICE: negatively as `forbidden = ["CommitTree promoted rec=", "CommitTree promoted chain-tip rec="]` (the exact Unfinished-Flights-opened signature) and positively as the REAP, which `IsReapEligible` makes reachable only when EVERY slot's tip is Immutable. **FLOWN ONCE 2026-08-05** (`2026-08-05_0725_GS-1-auto-chute-booster_a2`), INVALID(mission) both attempts - and the run PROVED the product half on its first try. **THE FIRST FLIGHT-AUTHORED REWINDPOINT IN THE SUITE'S HISTORY:** `Controllable split children: [2200110033,4093180920] (checked=2, unresolved=0)` -> `RewindPoint begin: rp=rp_48edec9... slots=2 controllablePids=2 focusSlot=0 ut=31.62` -> `slot capture: populated=2/2 disabled=0` -> `Wrote rp=... bytes=144646`, off a real decoupler split (`DECOUPLE emitted: ut=31.10 type=JointBreak splitCause=DECOUPLE controlledChildren=1 debris=0`) that cut the 15-part stack into a 2-part upper and a 13-part probe-cored booster. That also discharges assumptions A1-A4 of `build_gs1_craft.py` (the craft loads, flies and stages exactly as derived; `2200110033` in the log IS the persistentId the builder writes). **EVERY failure was in the mission driver**, all five fixed 2026-08-05 with regression cells: the missing COAST phase (the throttle cut is gated on the ORBIT apoapsis and the apex is ~9 s later, so it staged at 339 m climbing at 100 m/s instead of at the apex), a `chuteFullDeployAltMeters` of 1000 m sitting ABOVE the ~850 m apex (which made the arm-window give-up satisfiable from the pad - now 600 m AND gated on actually falling), a `separationObserved` row that was starved of frames rather than wrong (the next poll after the give-up read `avThr=0.000`; predicate unchanged, a `postStageThrustReads` diagnostic added), a `craftCanopyObserved value=null` that was the absent ARM ALTITUDE and not a stale chute stream (`chute=Stowed` reads on every frame; the runner re-enumerates parachutes live), and a latent unguarded LANDED terminal (KSP reported `situation = LANDED` at alt 230 m climbing at 113 m/s - the CL-1 defect class, closed before it could bite). **FLIGHT 2 2026-08-05** (`2026-08-05_0749`, attempt 1): **MISSION-OK in 107 s** - the COAST fix flew, the craft staged, and the commit route ran end to end, so all SEVEN group-3 commit tokens are now MEASURED on this lane (`exittospacecenter start/complete`, `CommitTreeSceneExit: stashed pending tree`, `Silent full-fidelity auto-commit (scene-exit): ... recordings=2 spawnable=1`, `Committed tree 'GS1 Auto-Chute Booster' (2 recordings)`, `CommitTree: promoted 1 normal staging RP(s)`, `OnSave: saving 1 committed tree(s)`). Verdict **PARSEK-FAIL(expectations)**, 3 mismatches - and the RED IS CORRECT. **The booster was destroyed in mid-air by a COLLISION WITH ITS OWN UPPER STAGE**, and every Parsek step after that was right: `IsUnfinishedFlight=true ... reason=crashed terminal=Destroyed` -> `CommitTree promoted rec=... reason=crashed to CommittedProvisional` -> `ReapOrphanedRPs: reaped=0 remaining=1` (`IsReapEligible` refuses while any slot tip is CommittedProvisional). A crashed booster SHOULD open an Unfinished Flight and SHOULD keep its RP; GS-1 asserts the other branch. **Root cause was this lane's OWN flight-1 fix**: putting COAST before STAGE moved the separation to the apex, where there is no airspeed and so no differential drag - the halves parted by 5.49 m, fell together (84.1 m vs 85.9 m over 3.6 s), and at ut 43.62 the upper stage was 2.0 m above the booster when `Decoupler.1 Exploded!!`. Fixed by reordering to ASCENT -> STAGE -> COAST -> DESCENT (separate with airspeed, then coast to the apex, then arm); full forensics in todo-and-known-bugs.md `GS1-APEX-SEPARATION-COLLISION`. **Also settled:** the reaper is NOT deferred to a later load - it ran 78 ms after the commit in the same SPACECENTER `OnLoad`, so the spec's timing model was right. `branchPoints` re-pinned `Breakup` -> `JointBreak` (the one genuine spec-side error). **Nothing was relaxed**: the two reap tokens and the forbidden `CommitTree promoted rec=` are the assertion this scenario exists to make. **FLIGHT 3 2026-08-05** (`2026-08-05_0807`): MISSION-OK, collision FIXED (booster recording 17 -> 146+ points), `branchPoints {JointBreak: 1}` matched. Still PARSEK-FAIL(expectations), on a THIRD distinct driver defect and again with Parsek correct: the mission's LANDED terminal watched only the ACTIVE vessel, so `ExitToSpaceCenter` fired while the booster was still under canopy and its recording closed `terminal=SubOrbital` -> `IsUnfinishedFlight=true ... reason=stableLeafUnconcluded slot=1 focusSlot=0 side=child` -> `CommitTree promoted rec=... reason=stableLeafUnconcluded to CommittedProvisional` -> no reap. On this craft the stage the mission does NOT fly lands LAST (six Mk2-R from a higher separation vs the pod's single Mk16). Fixed with a bounded **SIBLING-DOWN** phase plus a new roster-watch-shaped kRPC channel (`ACTION_SET_SIBLING_WATCH`, `TelemetrySnapshot.sibling_situation`/`.sibling_present` tri-state, `_read_sibling_situation` guarded so a faulted enumeration neither advances nor erases a streak); an ABSENT reading counts as destroyed only after the vessel was OBSERVED PRESENT, and a destroyed booster CONCLUDES the mission (stays MISSION-OK so the tail runs and the SPEC reds) rather than going driver-INVALID and discarding the evidence. Forensics in todo-and-known-bugs.md `GS1-SIBLING-STILL-FLYING`. **Measured Site A formats now pinned for reuse:** `CommitTree promoted rec=<id> vessel='<name>' slot=<n> rp=<rp> reason=<reason> to CommittedProvisional` with `reason=crashed` (flight 2) and `reason=stableLeafUnconcluded` (flight 3) - the token a lane whose child legitimately ends OPEN will want. **Nothing relaxed across three flights**: each red was a different driver defect and none a product one, which is the evidence the windows are pinned in the right place. **FLIGHT 4 2026-08-05 (`2026-08-05_0824`): PASS, attempt 1, 289 s - THE SCENARIO WORKS.** Every required token matched (`expectations status=PASS mismatches=[]`), including the two GROUP-4 REAP tokens that had never fired, and the forbidden `CommitTree promoted rec=` did not match. The save parse read **`rewindPoints: 0`** - the RewindPoint REAPED - plus `supersedeRows 0`, `tombstones 0`, `terminalStates {Landed: 2}`, `branchPoints {JointBreak: 1}`, `recordings 2`, `trees 1`, `committedTrees 1`. **A5 IS DISCHARGED**: `Landed: 2` means the booster survived touchdown under six Mk2-R, so the craft's computed ~4.3 m/s vs `fuelTankSmall`'s crashTolerance 6 held. This is the first flight-authored RewindPoint in the suite's history to be authored, committed and reaped in one run. **ARMED the same day** per the S4.1/CL-3 three-run discipline: 0824 was the report-only READING run (it ran `gating=False armed=[]` and every window was already met, so arming moved no verdict), then `[expectations.rewind] gating = true` + the `ARMED_ALLOWLIST` entry in `test_hlib.py::SaveStructureVerifierWiringTests`, with a negative control. `[expectations.recordings.structure]` stays REPORT-ONLY (single-sample windows). **Nothing was ever relaxed across four flights**: three reds, three distinct DRIVER defects, zero product defects - which is the evidence the windows were pinned in the right place all along | D1 auto-record-launch/commit-scene-exit/auto-merge; D5 controlled-decoupled-child; **D9 rp-disk-reaper** (first gate in the suite on the reaper against a physics-authored RP); D14 kerbin/sandbox/scene-flight/scene-ksc. `[expectations.rewind]` (`rewindPoints`/`supersedeRows`/`tombstones` all `max = 0`) is **ARMED** (`gating = true`) at HEAD, and the spec is in `ARMED_ALLOWLIST`. The S4.1/CL-3 three-run discipline was completed in full on 2026-08-05: reading run `0824` (`gating=False armed=[] mism=[]`), armed run `0832` (`gating=True armed=['rewind'] mism=[]`), then a NEGATIVE CONTROL `0836` which temporarily set `supersedeRows = {min = 1}` and correctly red PARSEK-FAIL(save-structure) `rewind.supersedeRows 0 < min 1` before being reverted. `[expectations.recordings.structure]` remains report-only |
| V4-player-loop-workflow | operator (calibration discipline) | THE PLAYER WORKFLOW end to end, unattended, and the FIRST consumer of the two player-loop seam verbs (`StartLoopPlayback` = the Missions window's "Warp to...", `EnterWatchMode` = "Watch"): load the committed duna-direct save, arm mission loop, warp to the engaged cycle-0 departure, press Watch, jump forward across the corrected cycle-0 Duna arrival seam (28,781,184.52, anchored to the D0 StartLoopPlayback itself echoes), and press Watch again at the ghost's parked Duna tail (D0 + 4,416,912.223). READING POSTURE, under-gated in exactly one place: both `EnterWatchMode` verdicts are PREDICTED `REJECTED` from recon-measured mechanism, not from a hunch - watch mode is CAMERA-ONLY with a 300 km entry / 305 km exit cutoff - which is the PRODUCTION range term and is correct as written (`ParsekFlight.cs:19580` -> `WatchModeController.cs:698` -> `lastDistance < WatchEnterCutoffMeters`); what is NOT correct is treating it as SUFFICIENT for entry, and V7M falsified that by refusing at 144-199 km and entering at 51.5 km (see the correction later in this row) - and an auto-select conjunction of `HasActiveGhost && IsGhostOnSameBody && IsGhostWithinVisualRange`. At the departure epoch the ghost is at KERBIN and the observer at Duna, so the body test alone refuses; at the parked tail both sit on the same ~1,038 km-radius orbit (this fixture's parked DD1: alt 718,363 m over Duna, SMA 1,038,214.95 / ecc 0.00127 - NOT V3C's 283 km park, whose radius an earlier draft imported by mistake), so only the chord decides - as WRITTEN that read "theta < ~16.6 deg, ~9% of a randomly-drawn phase after ~19.65 Ms of on-rails drift, hence REJECTED at ~10:1", and BOTH halves of that are superseded: the 300 km range term is not sufficient for entry, and against the entry boundary V7M actually MEASURED (bracketed in (51.5 km, 144.3 km); working figure ~120 km) the corrected condition is theta < 6.6 deg, and the draw model is dead - both craft advance at the same rate on the same orbit, so a separation is a fixed per-cycle geometric fact, not a re-roll. The REJECTED verdict stands with more margin than it was written with, and an OK there is still the more interesting measurement. The arrival-seam bracket is an EXPECTED-NULL kept because it is free: V3C measured the flight-engine hide gate at 120 km while the seam sits at Duna's 47,921,949 m SOI radius, ~400x over the gate, so zero seam samples from a parked observer is the correct outcome and a nonzero one would overturn that arithmetic | D9 fast-forward (gated by the product's own `FastForwardToEventUT: jumping to UT=` line, not by the seam's OK); D14 scene-flight/sandbox. NOT claimed: any watch-mode cell (a measured refusal to enter watch mode is not evidence of watch behaviour), D6 time-jump. **FLOWN 2026-08-08, run `2026-08-08_1135`, PASS ATTEMPT 1, wall 79 s, fully unattended, no spec iteration needed** - all 16 steps met, driverValidity/analyzer(red=0)/logValidate/expectations PASS, anomalySweep `hits=[] counts={}`, unityExceptions 0, saveParse REPORT clean. The whole player loop costs 79 s because every clock move is instantaneous (15,146,166 s in the warp, 4,474,434 s in the first bracket jump). THREE MEASUREMENTS. (1) THE WATCHABILITY ANSWER: both `EnterWatchMode` calls returned `REJECTED reason=no-watchable-ghost committed=3`, as predicted, but the two refusals differ and the second is the finding. At the launch epoch the ghost is on the Kerbin pad and the observer at Duna - categorical. At the parked tail the ghost was ACTIVE, at DUNA, playing its recorded park (`playbackUT=9159865.573` inside `endUT=9160152.704`), and the engine printed the term that failed: `GuardSkip ... reason=hidden-by-zone_distance=643913m` - 643.9 km, i.e. 2.15x outside the 300,000 m `WatchEnterCutoffMeters` the production range term compares `lastDistance` against, and 5.4x outside the entry boundary V7M later measured. **CORRECTION LEDGER, two passes, because this row was edited wrongly once.** PASS 1 (2026-08-08, V7M) established that "inside 300 km" is NOT SUFFICIENT for entry - V7M refused at 144-199 km and entered at 51.5 km. PASS 2 (2026-08-09) retracts the MECHANISM pass 1 attached to it: pass 1 wrote "watch ENTRY is `IsGhostWithinVisualRange` = `currentZone != Beyond`, i.e. the 120 km `GhostVisualRangeMeters` gate", and that method (`GhostPlaybackEngine.cs:6325`) has NO production caller - only `Parsek.Tests`. Production resolves the same name through `ParsekFlight.cs:19580` -> `WatchModeController.cs:698` to the 300 km / 305 km entry / exit cutoffs, so the range term cannot be what refuses inside 300 km, and WHICH conjunction term does is UNESTABLISHED (leading hypothesis and the discriminating experiment live in the single authority: `docs/dev/todo-and-known-bugs.md` -> WATCH-ENTRY-REFUSED-INSIDE-QUOTED-RANGE, formerly titled WATCH-ENTRY-GATE-IS-120KM-NOT-300KM). This lane's 643.9 km is outside both the range term and the boundary, so it discriminates nothing; the verdicts recorded here are unaffected by either pass. Two of the conjunction's three terms are PROVABLE from that log (HasActiveGhost via the hide-emit path, which only runs on a positioned active ghost; range 643,913 m vs 300,000) and the same-body term is INFERRED from `seedBody=Duna` on the same-frame director line, not read off a guard of its own. So watch mode is NOT categorically unreachable from a parked observer at the arrival tail; it missed by 2.15x on the range term on this phase draw, on a shared ~1,038 km-radius orbit whose maximum separation is ~2,077 km. (2) THE WARP-TO ANCHOR, corrected: `ComputeNextRelaunchUT` returns the mission's next RELAUNCH (the phase anchor, `relaunchUt=24306580.0709779`), NOT the interplanetary departure D0 (24,396,028.988, a separate `[Reaim]` value); the product jumped to 24,306,565.13 with the 15 s launch lead. Bracket UTs must anchor to the relaunch UT the verb echoes. On this fixture V2's raw offsets (measured against recording start 4,653,504.304) apply DIRECTLY to that anchor - V3C's -188.294 s correction is a matched pair with ITS anchor (24,306,768.36) and would double-count here. The 188.294 s is a SPAN-START-vs-RECORDING-START delta (4,653,692.598 - 4,653,504.304), NOT a pad-align shift: the PadAlignLaunch shift is +7,378.5 s in BOTH lanes, printed on V4's own log as `launchShift=7378.5s phaseAnchor=24306580.07` off `anchor=24299201.55`, and V3C's header never writes the 188.294 at all - it is implicit in that lane's span-start convention, which is how it got mis-attributed. Both routes land on 28,781,184.52 (phaseAnchor + raw offset = V3C's span-start-corrected identity), which is why the error was invisible. Confirmed independently by `[ReaimSeam] arrivalUT=28781185 soiEntryUT=28781185` and the icon driver's `bounds=[28781184.5,28813228.5]`. (3) THE SEAM BRACKET measured its expected null - zero `loop-seam-teleport`, zero anomalies of any reason, no `[ReaimSeam]` dist[] sampling at the seam instants - but ALSO qualified V3C's zone arithmetic: the one `hidden-by-zone` sample at the bracket read `distance=869479m`, 7.2x the 120 km gate, not the ~400x the 47.9 Mm SOI-radius argument predicts. ONE candidate survives and is FILED not softened: the icon driver's replay window starts AT the seam (`bounds=[28781184.5,28813228.5]`), so a pre-bounds ghost resolves to a clamped in-bounds position near Duna rather than to the SOI edge. The second candidate raised at the time - "the hide line prints `renderDistance`, cached separately from the `lastDistance` the watch conjunction reads" - is REFUTED: `ResolvePlaybackDistance` and `ResolvePlaybackActiveVesselDistance` (`GhostPlaybackEngine.cs:5693-5727`) are textually identical on the production path (the resolver overrides exist only for InGameTests) and one `CachePlaybackDistances` call writes both fields right before the hide emit, so in production renderDistance == lastDistance. That side effect STRENGTHENS measurement (1): `IsGhostWithinVisualRange` provably compared 643,913 against 300,000, not some other cached number **ARMED 2026-08-08 (the V2/B17 three-run discipline): both `[expectations.rewind]` and `[expectations.recordings.structure]` now carry `gating = true`.** READING `2026-08-08_1135` (PASS attempt 1) measured every declared window already met - rewindPoints 0, supersedeRows 0, tombstones 0, rewindRetirements 0, committedTrees 1, trees 1 - so the arming commit re-pinned no window (S4.1's rule). ARMED `2026-08-08_1154` PASS attempt 1, wall 50 s, saveParse `status=PASS gating=True armed=['rewind','recordings.structure'] mismatches=0`. NEGATIVE CONTROL `2026-08-08_1156` PARSEK-FAIL(save-structure) attempt 1, wall 50 s, the single mismatch `rewind.supersedeRows 0 < min 1` under a temporary `supersedeRows = { min = 1 }`, classifier `reason=gating save-structure expectations mismatch` - reverted immediately; the gate reds in the red direction. `trees` keeps V2's `max = 2` (the documented duplicate-writer hazard) deliberately WIDER than the measurement rather than being tightened at arming time. NOT armed: the armed run read `unityExceptions total=2 (NullReferenceException)` where the reading and control runs read 0 - two NREs on one of three otherwise-identical flights of the same fixture is a flake signature, and a count armed off a single observation is what this discipline exists to prevent. **MAP-PROBE ANTI-VACUITY PIN ADDED + RE-FLOWN 2026-08-09 (`2026-08-09_0927`, PASS attempt 1, wall 64 s, expectations mismatches=0).** This lane had been arming a Tier-C sweep (`mapRenderTracing` on, `allowedAnomalies = []`) while pinning NOTHING that proved the map probe's per-pid lens ever reached a resolved ghost. The obvious pin - `probe frame summary ... ghosts=[1-9] sampled=[1-9]`, V1's and V2's token - is a rate-limiter race at this spec shape and loses it every time here: `ParsekLog.VerboseRateLimited` holds that summary on a 5.0 s WALL-CLOCK, process-global shared key, the first call lands +3 ms after the tracer flips with no ghost resolved yet (`frame=6016 ghosts=0 sampled=0`), and the whole tracer-on dwell is 2.513 s. All seven archived tracer-on runs of this family read `ghosts=0 sampled=0` on dwells of 2.246-3.772 s; V1 buys the token honestly only through its 33-minute dwell. PINNED INSTEAD: `faithful-parity summary sampled=\d+ overTolerance=\d+` (PRESENCE - the `MapRenderProbe.LateUpdate` emit is gated on `sampled > 0 || skipCounts.Count > 0`, so it only appears when the per-pid pass actually reached a resolved ghost), with the S1.6/S1.7 `overTolerance=[1-9][0-9]*` form forbidden. NOT pinned, `sampled=[1-9]`: this lane reads `sampled=0 skip.reaimed-or-foreign-seed=1`, and the re-flight showed that is NOT the designed re-aim skip - the parked Duna tail is a FAITHFUL leg (re-aim applies per segment), the seed is recorded segment 12 fed verbatim and the covering is raw segment 15 of the same conic, i.e. `FAITHFUL-PARITY-SEED-FRAGMENT-REBASE` in the todo doc. The new report-only `seam-endpoint-outside-soi` encounter lens read ZERO raises here and did NOT gate (correct report-only registration), and the sentence that used to follow - "structurally so, every sampled frame's covering segment is the recording's LAST, so the instrument skips `no-cross-body-successor`; this lane does not yet exercise it" - was an OFFLINE DERIVATION and is now MEASURED WRONG. **SEAM-ENDPOINT CENSUS 2026-08-09** (`_1015` reading, `_1028` + `_1036` re-flights, all PASS attempt 1, expectations mismatches=0): this lane reads `seam-endpoint summary evaluated=1 outsideSoi=0` with NO skip bucket, bit-identical on all three. It did so on the very frame where the faithful-parity sibling stood down (`skip.reaimed-or-foreign-seed=1`, Director seed `epoch+shift=NaN`), which is the instrument's not-re-aim-gated design paying off. This lane and V7M are the two of the five that buy the instrument. PINNED from the census: `seam-endpoint summary evaluated=\d+ outsideSoi=\d+` (presence) AND `seam-endpoint summary evaluated=[1-9]` (the lens actually performed a check) - the latter under the two-consecutive-flights rule, proven on a third. NOT pinned: `outsideSoi=0` (the instrument's documented benign population). **RE-FLOWN 2026-08-09 `_1158` (PASS attempt 1, wall 76 s, expectations mismatches=0) after the capture gained the re-timed-arrival refusal** (`skip.reaimed-seam-instant-unknown`; see the M-06 entry's limit (3)): reading UNCHANGED at `evaluated=1 outsideSoi=0`, the new skip bucket absent from the log, zero anomalies. This lane is the one that proves the refusal is narrow enough not to cost a measurement - its sample is a re-aim owner on the DIRECT tof path, where the seed's own end IS the recorded seam. |
| V5-ts-loop-arrival | operator (calibration discipline) | THE FIRST TRACKING-STATION OBSERVATION of a looped, re-aimed, INTERPLANETARY ghost. V2 dwelled the FLIGHT-scene map camera over this fixture and H23 proved a driven boot into TRACKSTATION; nothing had put the two together, and the TS scene has its own ghost host (`ParsekTrackingStation`, its own ProtoVessel lifecycle and marker/orbit-line surface). The step chain is SHAPED BY A PRECONDITION, not by preference: `MissionConfig`, `StartLoopPlayback` and `TimeJump` are all `RequiresFlight`, so arming and clock work happen in FLIGHT, are persisted by `SaveGame` (ghost ProtoVessels stripped on save by `GhostMapPresence.StripFromSave`), and only then re-entered via `LoadGame scene=trackstation` at 135.5 s past the corrected cycle-0 arrival seam - the first instant a TS observer sees a looped interplanetary member inside the destination SOI. THE DWELL IS SHORT AND SAID TO BE: no seam verb passes time in TS, so it is the load settle plus two `RecordingState` poll ticks - enough for "does the TS host materialize the member at all", not for a temporal question. Anti-vacuity rides the TS host's OWN `ParsekTrackingStation initialized: created N ghost vessel(s)` line with N >= 1; the probe-summary pin is kept but labelled WEAK in the spec because the probe also runs in FLIGHT and its line carries no scene token | D14 scene-ts/sandbox. NOT claimed: D11 arrival cells (this observes an arrival epoch, it asserts no arrival geometry), D6 ghost-map-presence (H23/H28's contract), D9 fast-forward (V4 owns it). **FLOWN 2026-08-08, THREE ATTEMPTS ACROSS TWO RUNS, GREEN on the second run's attempt 1 (`2026-08-08_1144`, PASS, wall 50 s, fully unattended)** - all 16 steps met, driverValidity/analyzer(red=0)/logValidate/expectations PASS, anomalySweep `hits=[] counts={}`, unityExceptions 0, saveParse REPORT clean. THE ANSWER IS POSITIVE: a looped, RE-AIMED, interplanetary member DOES materialize in the tracking station at its arrival epoch - `ParsekTrackingStation initialized: created 1 ghost vessel(s)`, the Director assembling `factory chain ... phases=7 reaimed=True`, `MapRenderTrace phase=GhostCreated surface=ProtoIcon ... body=Duna scene=TRACKSTATION`, and a `GhostMap Created ghost vessel ... body=Duna sma=-137764 ecc=8.536203 ... orbitRenderer=True rendererEnabled=True drawIcons=ALL visibilityReason=visible scene=TRACKSTATION` - i.e. a Duna-framed INBOUND HYPERBOLA with a live orbit renderer and an unsuppressed icon, drawn from the `visible-segment` source over the recorded 9,128,153.3-9,159,555.6 window, with the debris member correctly skipped. TWO ITERATION FINDINGS from run 1 (both spec-side, both now fixed and documented in the header). (a) THE PROMOTION-RECORDER RE-ARM RACE: run 1 attempt 1 died `reject LoadGame reason=recording-active` because the scene-entry promotion recorder re-armed a SECOND time after the first StopRecording (measured: start 50.294 -> stop 50.599 -> second start 50.842), while attempt 2 on the identical spec saw no second start - same fixture, opposite outcomes. Mitigated with a re-kill pair immediately before the TS load, which is the only placement the guard cares about. **SEEN AGAIN 2026-08-09, and the word "deterministically" (which is what this said) is retracted:** V7T - which inherited the re-kill pair verbatim - lost `_1020` attempt 1 to the identical `reject id=0013 cmd=LoadGame reason=recording-active`, with `ResumeCommittedActiveRecording` re-arming 227 ms after the pair's DiscardTree. So the pair narrows the window; it does not close it. Second sighting recorded, not re-diagnosed, per this lane's own instruction; filed as TS-LOADGAME-RECORDING-ACTIVE-RACE in `docs/dev/todo-and-known-bugs.md`. (b) THE PROBE PIN IS UNPINNABLE BY THIS SPEC SHAPE: `probe frame summary` is VerboseRateLimited at 5.0 s on a shared key, no seam verb passes time in TRACKSTATION, and the harness drives ~0.25 s per step - the run quit 1.08 s after the TS ghost appeared (first TS ghost 14:44:43.060 -> `Application.Quit` 14:44:44.140 on run `2026-08-08_1144`; an earlier draft said 2.2 s, and the measured figure only strengthens the point), inside the limiter window, and the only probe line in the log was a FLIGHT-half `ghosts=0`. That pin (which could never discriminate scene anyway - the line carries no scene token) was REPLACED by two TS/mode-stamped tokens, a strengthening rather than a relaxation. A real TS probe reading needs a TS-legal time-passing verb, which does not exist. FLAKE LEDGER: quarantined at rate 0.50 from run 1's INVALID + PARSEK-FAIL; both were the spec-side findings above, fixed, and run 2 was clean on attempt 1 **ARMED 2026-08-08, same discipline: both save-structure blocks now carry `gating = true`.** READING `2026-08-08_1144` (PASS attempt 1) measured every window already met - rewindPoints 0, supersedeRows 0, tombstones 0, rewindRetirements 0, committedTrees 1, trees 1, i.e. the DOUBLE scene entry produced no second committed tree and the mid-run SaveGame persisted no stub. ARMED `2026-08-08_1155` PASS attempt 1, wall 51 s, all 16 steps met, saveParse `status=PASS gating=True armed=['rewind','recordings.structure'] mismatches=0`, unityExceptions 0. The arming is worth more here than on a single-scene dwell: V5 is the ONE committed spec that writes a save mid-run and reads it back through a second scene load, the shape in which a stray durable write is easiest to miss. NEGATIVE CONTROL flown once, on V4 (`2026-08-08_1156`); both specs gate through the one shared saveParse evaluator, so a second identical inversion would re-prove the evaluator rather than these windows, at the cost of a flight. The structure-block half is un-inverted on both specs - that is the honest limit of what the two controls demonstrate. |

| V6M-mun-player-loop | operator (calibration discipline) | THE PLAYER WORKFLOW ON A SAME-PARENT MOON MISSION, FLOWN TWICE OVER - the first lane in the suite that runs the same loop through two cycles to ask whether cycle N still arrives where cycle 1 did, and the first to pin the OTHER routing road. A Kerbin->Mun mission is always phaseLocked, so `ApplyReaim` (`MissionLoopUnitBuilder.cs:453-466`, gated on `!phaseLocked`) is structurally unreachable: the whole V4/V5 re-aim vocabulary is FORBIDDEN here (`[ReaimDiag]`, `ENGAGED re-aim`, `FORCED FAITHFUL`) and the positive pins replace it. **FLOWN 2026-08-08, run `2026-08-08_1554`, PASS ATTEMPT 1, wall 54 s, fully unattended, no spec iteration** - all 21 steps met, driverValidity/analyzer(red=0)/logValidate/expectations PASS, anomalySweep `hits=[] counts={}`, unityExceptions 0, saveParse REPORT clean. FOUR MEASUREMENTS. | S4.2-refly-world-preservation | nightly | THE ONLY LIVE PROOF that an invoked Re-Fly leaves the unrelated world standing (REFLY-DELETES-NON-SLOT-WORLD / audit C1) - and IT PROVED IT, on its first flight, while going red on an unrelated token. Drives S4.1's seam cycle over a NEW fixture whose RP quicksave carries an unrelated fleet (the gloops-airshow donor's own `Ast. VPA-167` SpaceObject re-admitted verbatim with its DISCOVERY block, a Station, a Flag, and a Probe + Debris pair BOTH named `WP Booster A` after the crashed booster recording), then runs the 6-cell `ReFlyWorldPreservation` in-game batch WHILE the session is live, against the RP quicksave on disk classified with the production scrub's own extracted predicate. WHY S4.1 COULD NOT COVER IT: every other rewind fixture's RP sidecar holds one vessel per slot and nothing else, so "the fleet survived" was vacuously true and S4.1 flew green through every day the bug was live. **FLOWN 2026-08-11, run `2026-08-11_1057`, attempt 1, wall 63 s, fully unattended, PARSEK-FAIL(expectations) ON EXACTLY ONE TOKEN.** All 7 driver steps met; driverValidity / batchComplete / analyzer(red=0) / logValidate / anomalySweep(`hits=[] counts={}`) PASS, unityExceptions REPORT total=0, saveParse REPORT clean (rewindPoints 1, supersedeRows 0, tombstones 0, pointsTotal 12, mismatches 0). THE PRESERVATION QUESTION CAME BACK POSITIVE AND IS THE POINT OF THE LANE: `BATCH_COMPLETE v1 total=6 passed=6 failed=0 skipped=0 category=ReFlyWorldPreservation scene=FLIGHT` - the DERIVED tally held TOKEN FOR TOKEN, and the derivation the spec flagged as able to move did not move (`Marker valid ... inPlace=True`, so the #587 positive control ran rather than skipping). The six cells measured, verbatim: `Preserved-world check: rp=rp_wp_root expectedPresent=4 missing=0 debrisExempt=1 liveRealVessels=5`; `Sibling-removal check: selectedInScene=1/1 siblings=1 survivingSiblings=0`; `#587 non-debris survival check: nameCollisions=1 removed=0 killEligibleNames=3`; `#587 debris-kill positive control: debrisNameMatches=1 survivors=0` (BOTH directions of the discrimination, which is what stops a #587 pass that had simply stopped firing from reading green); `Flag/SpaceObject check: flags=1 spaceObjects=1 missing=0`; and the advisory composing over live state. The independent regression pin also matched: `Re-Fly save scrub applied: rp=rp_wp_root slot=1 vesselsBefore=7 kept=6 preserved=5 removed=1 activeVessel=0` - a pre-fix DLL emits `preserved=0`, so the token discriminates the fix from its absence directly. **THE FINDING, and it is about the CONCLUSION rather than the preservation: `AppendRelations outcome=refused-unflown-provisional` never appeared, because the merge never reached `SupersedeCommit.AppendRelations` at all.** The spec derived that a rewind-then-conclude-without-flying would reach that refusal (the branch `SupersedeCommit.cs:248-259` takes when `ValidateSupersedeTarget` rejects a trajectory-less provisional, and whose own comment names "rewind, then end the session without flying" as a normal-play route). What the product actually did, in order: `CommitTreeSceneExit (autoMerge off)` -> an ORDINARY whole-tree merge over 3 recordings with the provisional EXCLUDED (`ERS Rebuilt: 2 entries from 3 committed ... skippedSuppressed=1`) -> `WARN [MergeDialog] TryCommitReFlySupersede: provisional rec=rec_cf0ed609... not found in committed list after tree commit; leaving marker in place for load-time sweep` -> on the NEXT load, `WARN [ReFlySession] Marker invalid field=ActiveReFlyRecordingId; cleared ... rejected because active recording was not found in RecordingStore.CommittedRecordings` -> LoadTimeSweep, ending `marker=False ... supersedes=0`. So the conclusion is retired by the ZOMBIE-PROVISIONAL SWEEP, one layer below the refusal the pin expects. END STATE IS BENIGN AND CONVERGENT - 0 supersede rows, origin stays effective, no non-convergent reload loop, which is also what the refusal branch produces - so this is a ROUTE difference, not a corruption; that is exactly why it must be judged rather than pattern-widened away. **AND THE RUN ESTABLISHES A ROUTE THAT `.claude/CLAUDE.md` RECORDS AS UNESTABLISHED.** R1-EMPTY-PROVISIONAL's observation-only raise fired on a real driven flight: `WARN [ReFlySession] outcome=unbound-refly-provisional reason=refly-provisional-has-no-trajectory-at-save sess=... provisional=rec_cf0ed609... treeKind=pending tree -- this session's provisional is being saved with no trajectory sidecar ... If the session concludes in this state the merge writes 0 supersede rows and the origin branch stays effective`. The prediction in that message came true to the letter. `ReFlyProvisionalBinding` is documented as OBSERVATION ONLY on the grounds that "the route to that state is not established; the only demonstrated route was fixture-shaped" - this flight is a driven, production-shaped RP sidecar reaching it, so that sentence now has a counter-example and the honest reading is that the route IS reachable by rewind-then-conclude. NOTE the timing subtlety before concluding the provisional is empty at merge: it is empty at the mid-session save (the raise above) and has FOUR points by the tail (`PRE_REFLY_ANCHOR written: rec=rec_cf0ed609... points=4`), accumulated while the batch ran, so "unflown" and "trajectory-less" are NOT the same predicate here - which may be the whole reason `ValidateSupersedeTarget` was never the gate that fired. NOT RETRIED (a PARSEK-FAIL that reached a verdict already survived run.py's retry policy), NOT budgeted, and the required token deliberately LEFT IN PLACE | D9 rewind-to-separation/refly-gate/reconciliation-bundle/read-back-guard + **world-preservation** (new registry value, added with this spec, and the only cell this spec uniquely owns - claimed off the batch, which passed 6/6); D8 recalc-engine; D14 sandbox/scene-flight. **D9 `merge-journal` NOW CARRIES A MEASURED CAVEAT**: this flight never opened one (`journal=False` on every OnSave/OnLoad, `MergeJournal Journal saved: none`), because the tail bailed before the orchestrator - the claim is left standing rather than silently dropped so the call is a human one, and the spec header says so too. NOT claimed: supersede-relation and head-tip-split (zero rows by design - nothing flies between rewind and conclusion), terminal-kind-classify (S4.1 owns it), tombstones (uncrewed booster). Both save-structure blocks deliberately REPORT-ONLY and NOT armed - V7T's posture: arming a second gate on a lane whose verdict already carries a finding pins windows no green run ever confirmed. `pending-operator` tag was KEPT at that point, on S4.1's own rule that it stays until the first green run (DROPPED on the confirming re-fly below). **FIX LANDED 2026-08-11 (branch `refly-conclusion-route`); the lane stayed in RED BY FINDING until the supervisor re-flew it, which is the confirming run recorded at the end of this row.** The finding's open question - which of the two routes is intended - was the wrong question: both routes occur on the SAME shape and are separated by LOAD HISTORY, not policy. The provisional enters the flat committed list once (`RewindInvoker.AddProvisional` at invoke) and nothing puts it back; S4.1 runs the identical `PruneZeroPointLeaves: removed 1 zero-point leaf` on its own provisional and still reaches the refusal purely because nothing reloaded in between, while THIS run's in-game-batch isolation revert re-hydrated the provisional into `tree.Recordings` ONLY (`ReconcileInPlaceForkIntoTreeIfNeeded: fork rec=... absent from committed list but present in tree.Recordings`), after which the prune emptied that surface too. For a player the same sequence is any F5/F9 during an open Re-Fly, so this was never fixture-shaped. Severity is bounded by the fact that the prune only ever removed recordings `ValidateSupersedeTarget` would have refused anyway - no route to that bail could drop a supersede that was owed - so what was defective is the ROUTE, not the outcome. THE FIX: the leaf prune keeps a provisional the merge would accept and hands over one it would refuse (`outcome=refly-provisional-prune-kept` / `outcome=retired-empty-provisional`), and `MergeDialog.TryCommitReFlySupersede` classifies the absence and runs `AppendRelations` on the handed-over recording, so the pinned `AppendRelations outcome=refused-unflown-provisional` fires in-session and the marker clears there (`outcome=concluded-no-supersede`) instead of on the next load. No merge journal is opened on that route BY DESIGN (zero rows, zero tombstones, no MergeState flip - the only mutation is the marker clear, whose crash fallback is the pre-fix behaviour LoadTimeSweep already handles), so **the D9 `merge-journal` caveat above STANDS: this lane still never opens one**, and that is now a deliberate property rather than a bail. THE TIMING SUBTLETY IS RESOLVED: the tail's `PRE_REFLY_ANCHOR ... points=4` is a copy of the ORIGIN's pre-rewind tail in `PreReFlyAnchorPoints`, not the attempt's own flight (`FinalizeTreeRecordings: rec='rec_cf0ed...' points=0`), so the refusal WOULD have fired with `reason=empty Points` - and the refusal line now prints `points= orbitSegments= trackSections= playableSections= preReFlyAnchorPoints= terminal=` so nobody reconstructs that again. SPEC RE-PINNED to the post-fix contract, all three conclusion tokens marked DERIVED-PENDING-REFLY, with the two pre-fix cascade lines (`not found in committed list after tree commit`, `Marker invalid field=ActiveReFlyRecordingId`) moved to `forbidden`. Unit coverage `Source/Parsek.Tests/ReFlyConclusionRouteTests.cs` (20 cells; suite 20193 passed / 1 skipped, `harness/lib` 1284 OK) - NOT a substitute for the flight, which is what re-measures the three derived tokens. **CONFIRMING RE-FLY FLOWN 2026-08-12, run `2026-08-11_2111`, attempt 2, wall 62 s: PASS - THE LANE IS GREEN AND LEAVES THIS SECTION.** Every verifier PASS or REPORT (driverValidity PASS, batchComplete PASS `perCategory=1`, analyzer red=0, logValidate PASS, anomalySweep `hits=[] counts={}`, unityExceptions REPORT total=0, expectations PASS **mismatches=0**, saveParse REPORT `supersedeRows=0 tombstones=0 rewindPoints=1 pointsTotal=12 mismatches=0`), zero `[Parsek][ERROR]` lines. The batch re-read `BATCH_COMPLETE v1 total=6 passed=6 failed=0 skipped=0 category=ReFlyWorldPreservation scene=FLIGHT` and the scrub pin re-read `preserved=5 removed=1 activeVessel=0`, so the preservation proof reproduced. THE THREE DERIVED CONCLUSION TOKENS ARE NOW MEASURED and none of the text moved: `[ReFlySession] outcome=retired-empty-provisional ... prune=PruneZeroPointLeaves`, `[Supersede] AppendRelations outcome=refused-unflown-provisional ... reason=empty Points ... points=0 preReFlyAnchorPoints=4 terminal=Landed`, `[Supersede] ConcludeRetiredProvisional outcome=concluded-no-supersede ... retireReason=PruneZeroPointLeaves rows=0 tombstones=0`. Both pre-fix cascade lines stayed ABSENT (0 occurrences each), which is what turns "the sweep route is not taken" into a measurement. Note `reason=empty Points` alongside `preReFlyAnchorPoints=4`: the refusal fires on the attempt's OWN zero points, not the origin's copied anchor tail - the timing subtlety the first flight could not resolve. The D9 `merge-journal` CAVEAT STANDS unchanged: this route opens no journal BY DESIGN. **ATTEMPT 1 WAS INVALID(driver) `subkind=seam-timeout`** - `AnswerMergeDialog` deferred 120 s on `reason=no-refly-dialog` because `[ReFlySession] End reason=treeDiscarded` had fired ~1.2 s after `Started`, during the batch's own baseline restore; its batch was still green 6/6. That is the quicksave-during-a-live-session race the spec's driver note flagged as having no committed precedent, NOT a conclusion-route regression - `flake.json` quarantines the scenario at `rate=0.50 over 7d` and the discriminator on a repeat is whether `End reason=treeDiscarded` precedes the defer. `pending-operator` tag DROPPED on this green run per the entry's own rule (`DROPPED_2026_08_12` in `harness/lib/test_hlib.py`). Both save-structure blocks REMAIN report-only and UNARMED - that is a separate operator decision this run does not make. |

| FORGE-b18-dres-pad | operator | FIXTURE-FORGE, not a coverage flight, and the SIXTH consumer of `forge_station` + `harvest_bdock_station.py` (no new mission, no new tool). Boots the committed `bdock-forge-base` and launches the NEW committed `Duna Rocket.craft` onto the pad with one named crew, settling PRELAUNCH; `harvest_bdock_station.py --run-save bdock-forge-base --target-name b18-dres-pad --expect-situation PRELAUNCH` then stamps the `b18-dres-pad` fixture. THE CRAFT is NOT authored by construction (the GS-1 / B17 builder pattern does not apply): it is a downloaded pure-stock 92-part KerbalX craft (Steltuck, `Duna Rocket`, KSP 1.12.1) committed VERBATIM -- CONTENT-identical to its download and LF-NORMALISED on commit, so there are TWO hashes: `f504608c...9328` AS COMMITTED (213,198 bytes, 0 CRLF, 12,804 LF) and `f664d7ce...5fc2` for the CRLF form the download arrives in (226,002 bytes). Both sites used to print only the latter under the words "byte-identical to its download", which was FALSE as written -- it matches no committed file (FORGE-CRAFT-BYTE-IDENTICAL-CLAIM-IS-FALSE-AS-WRITTEN in todo-and-known-bugs.md). The LF form is stable everywhere because `.gitattributes:30` marks `harness/fixtures/** -text`, and NOTHING GATES EITHER HASH, so the craft file itself is the derivation record. No MechJeb edit -- capability is install-side via the provisioner's MM patch, and a `mechjeb` grep over the craft returns zero hits like every other committed fixture craft. **FLOWN TWICE 2026-08-11**: run `_2125` PASS attempt 1 (105 s) stamped a fixture with an EMPTY POD, and B18 flight 1 then sat at PRE_LAUNCH with throttle 0.000 for its entire ascent phase with MechJeb engaged. ROOT CAUSE, measured: in `bdock-forge-base` **Jebediah is already `state = Assigned`** (he crews the base's own active vessel), so `launch_vessel(crew=['Jebediah Kerman'])` cannot seat him -- and it does not fail loudly, it launches an empty pod and leaves him `state = Missing`. The committed `gs1-two-stage-pad` fixture carries the SAME empty pod and the SAME Missing Jeb; GS-1 never noticed because its craft pairs a probeCoreOcto2 with its Mk1 pod and is probe-controlled. The Duna Rocket has exactly one ModuleCommand and no probe core, so an empty pod is fatal to it. | B20-moho-orbit | operator (calibration discipline) | THE MOHO PROGRAM'S STEP 1, and the flight whose PRODUCT IS THE RECORDING: Kerbin 700 km park -> pre-transfer JETTISON -> ejection at the Kerbin-to-Moho window -> heliocentric coast with corrections -> CAPTURE inside Moho's SOI -> held park -> mid-mission CommitTree, to be harvested into the `moho-orbit-recorded` fixture that V11 / V11A then read. NO new machinery: B19's JETTISON phase is already shared and default-off, so this lane is a body name plus re-derived sizing over the same `b5_decide`. WHY MOHO: it is the re-aim synthesizer's OWN DOCUMENTED FAILURE CASE (7 deg inclination, ecc ~0.2 -- the population its comments place beyond Duna's 0.06, Eve's 2.1 and Dres's 5). THE SIZING MOHO FORCES, by arithmetic: `approachMaxWarpFactor` 5 -> 4, because Moho's SOI is 9,646,663 m against Dres's 32,832,840 and 2*mu/r_soi = 34,957 m^2/s^2 is negligible against a v_inf^2 of 5.5e6-2.0e7, so the passage is near straight-line and the SOI-entry to periapsis coast is 2,168-4,119 game s against Dres's MEASURED ~25,000; B19's rule (one frame at most 1/5 of the coast, carried as arithmetic in `test_one_frame_cannot_swallow_the_dres_approach`) then FAILS at x1,000 and passes at x100. `soiLeadSeconds` deliberately does NOT scale with it -- that knob is sized against the COAST phase's single-frame advance (B19 flight 4 measured 27,596 game s in one poll), a function of coastWarpFactor and poll rate rather than of the target. FLOWN TWICE 2026-08-12 (`_1628` and its retry `_1704`); BOTH INVALID(autopilot-flake) on `MISSION-FLAKE reason=phase COAST-TO-TARGET timed out`, the two heliocentric orbits agreeing to the 8th significant figure. THE MEASURED CAUSE, and it is THIS LANE'S OWN PARAMETER rather than a product or planner defect: MechJeb planned an encounter-CREATING correction of 1,089.5-1,090.8 m/s and the lane's first-authored `maxCorrectionDvMps = 700` REMOVED it -- `course-correction dv 1089.5 m/s exceeds cap 700.0; plan removed (correction disqualified, coast will fly the raw intercept)` -- six times per flight, in both attempts. The coast then flew the raw intercept and timed out. 1,090 is BELOW B19's untouched 1200, so the proven value would have flown it. The 700 came from deriving the correction's size from a dv reserve instead of measuring it, and B15's lesson (a cap below the needed burn silently discards the node) is exactly what happened; the spec had even predicted the signature and named the cap as the first thing to re-read. RESTORED to 1200; re-fly pending. The transfer's own diagnostic is real and still worth having (`reachesTargetOrbit=reaches gap=0 m` with `targetSoiEncounterPredicted=NO`, the ejection coming out `inc=0.007` against Moho's 7 deg) -- that is WHY the correction costs ~1,090 rather than the tens of m/s a low-inclination lane needs, but it is the correction's SIZE, not an inability to plan one. INVALID rather than PARSEK-FAIL by the orthogonality rule: the mission did not fly, so nothing past the Kerbin-to-Sun handoff exercised a Parsek surface and no Parsek claim is made or void. WHAT DID WORK, all measured: the 700 km park (769,845 x 769,649 m, ecc ~1e-4); JETTISON stopping at TWO pops and handing off at `avThr=60000.000`, reproducing B19's Skipper-live path on the identical craft; the ejection node `nodeDv=1735.502`, which is +4.5% over the ~1,661 COPLANAR derivation and NOT evidence the 7 deg was folded in (it was not); and 5,724 m/s STILL IN THE STAGE after ejection -- at the restored cap that leaves ~4,434 m/s realistic against a worst-case ~3,862 capture, so THE CRAFT IS NOT THE PROBLEM. Finding recorded as CORRECTION-CAP-DERIVED-BELOW-THE-PLANNED-CORRECTION in todo-and-known-bugs. BLOCKS `moho-orbit-recorded` and the V11 / V11A tilt lanes until the re-fly. THIRD FLIGHT `_1855`, cap restored to 1200: the correction FLEW (1,089.5 m/s down to a clean `corrGiveup ->cut-reached nodeDv=1.751`) and CREATED the encounter -- `nextPe` went from nan to a real value -- so the lane REACHED MOHO'S SOI for the first time. Still INVALID(autopilot-flake), now on `capture-never-armed (past-periapsis)`: the second finding, APPROACH-WARP-CLAMP-FAILS-OPEN-ON-AN-INTERMITTENT-TTS. The clamp WORKED (stair-down 88 -> 22 -> 10 -> 3.4 -> 1x, halting at `tts=100000.353`, i.e. `soiLeadSeconds` to the digit -- the sizing is vindicated), then `tts` returned nan, `mlib.approach_warp_clamp`'s deliberate fail-open dropped the ceiling, and ONE frame advanced 55,041 game s through the lead, the boundary and the whole coast. THE GEOMETRY WAS RIGHT: the first in-SOI frame read `pe=450629.528 ttPe=+3154.010`, a 200 km periapsis altitude with periapsis 3,154 s AHEAD -- this flight was one warp frame from a capture. The proposed latch is machinery on a function every interplanetary lane shares, so it is FILED rather than taken unilaterally. LIVE-PROVEN 2026-08-12 (`_2331`, PASS attempt 1): Moho orbit 526.7 x 510.1 km, ecc 0.0108, capture node 2,639.9 m/s executed by MechJeb's NodeExecutor, ~1,991 m/s of the measured 7,483 still in the stage, all six mission assertions met, terminalOrbitBody=Moho. FIVE flights to get there and only ONE of the three defects was in shared machinery: (1) CORRECTION-CAP-DERIVED-BELOW-THE-PLANNED-CORRECTION, this lane's own derived cap discarding the 1,090 m/s encounter-creating burn six times a flight; (2) APPROACH-WARP-CLAMP-FAILS-OPEN-ON-AN-INTERMITTENT-TTS, the shared clamp dropping its ceiling on a blinking tts and letting one frame cover 55,041 game s past periapsis; (3) the latch fixing (2) initially engaging on ANY SOI boundary, caught in clean-context review before it flew. All three fixed and recorded in todo-and-known-bugs. The DERIVED sizing was vindicated: the stair-down halted at `tts=100000.353` (soiLeadSeconds to the digit) and `approachMaxWarpFactor = 4` sampled the approach at ~50 game s/poll as designed. Wall 4,791 s against a predicted ~4,750. | D1 auto-record-launch + commit-in-foreign-soi; D3 orbital-checkpoint; D4 atmospheric / exo-propulsive / exo-ballistic / cohesive-cross-body-coast; D14 kerbin/moho/soi-count/warp-rails/warp-high/scene-flight/sandbox. NOT claimed: D3 parent-anchored-debris (B19's carve-out, kept). The recordings count is RE-PINNED to the measured 5, named by UT in the fixture's provenance block; the pre-flight {3,12} window did its job and is closed. The approach sizing is now CORROBORATED rather than derived: the clamp stair-down halted at tts=100000.353, soiLeadSeconds to the digit, and factor 4 sampled the approach at ~50 game s per poll |
