| B21-eeloo-orbit | operator (calibration discipline) | EELOO PROGRAM STEP 1 -- B19's flown Dres profile RETARGETED: 700 km Kerbin park, PRE-TRANSFER JETTISON onto the LV-N, ejection at the Kerbin->Eeloo window, heliocentric coast with two SCHEDULED correction rounds, CAPTURE inside Eeloo's SOI, held park, mid-mission CommitTree, then FlushAndQuit so the produced save carries the whole committed tree. **FLOWN GREEN TWICE 2026-08-12** -- `2026-08-12_2003` (PASS attempt 1, 3,083 s wall) and `2026-08-12_2239` (PASS attempt 1, 3,092 s wall / 3,020 s mission), each with all six mission assertions met and every verifier PASS/SKIPPED/REPORT. **THE COMMITTED FIXTURE `eeloo-orbit-recorded` IS `_2239`'s OUTPUT, NOT `_2003`'s**: `_2003`'s produced save was destroyed 53 s after it ended by sibling scenario `B20-moho-orbit` starting a run against the same `saveTemplate` leaf, so every pin must be re-measured against `_2239` (filed as HARNESS-PRODUCED-SAVE-CLOBBERED-BY-SIBLING-RUN in todo-and-known-bugs.md). NO PARAMETER NEEDED RE-TUNING: the flight was green at the authored values and not one was changed on the re-pin. WHICH END OF THE BAND: the heliocentric coast ran ejection ut 5,096,457.1 -> Eeloo SOI entry ut 46,968,150.4 = **41,871,693.3 game s, 0.900 of the 46.51e6 APHELION figure** and 1.22x the 34.27e6 SMA one, so the arrival landed near the aphelion end of the 23.34/34.27/46.51e6 band the header sized against -- sizing on the worst case rather than the middle is what this flight vindicates. TWO CORRECTIONS OF THE HEADER'S OWN ARITHMETIC, both found by measuring rather than by re-reading. (1) **`coastTimeoutSeconds` BOUNDS ONE COAST-TO-TARGET SEGMENT, NOT THE WHOLE COAST** (`snapshot.ut - state.phase_entry_ut`, mlib.py:8111-8116, re-stamped on every phase re-entry): three segments measured, 80,750.0 / **23,534,341.5** / 6,743,381.1 game s. B19's inherited 16e6 WOULD still have reaped this flight -- 68% into segment 2, at ut 32,686,411, before round 2 ever planned -- so the widen was genuinely load-bearing; but the rejected 42e6 would have carried **1.78x** the worst segment, not the 1.003x a whole-coast reading implies, and the chosen 60e6 carries 2.55x. (2) **THE APPROACH MODEL IS FLIGHT-VALIDATED, BUT NOT BY THE NUMBER AN EARLIER DRAFT OF THIS ROW QUOTED.** The first in-SOI frame reported `ttPe=+69,895.611` -- POSITIVE, periapsis ahead, the clamp doing exactly its job. That row previously read the header's hyperbolic-Kepler re-derivation (measured arrival elements: pe altitude 11,343,134.761 m, ecc 447.020 => abs(a) 25,902.728 m, v_inf 1,694.905 m/s) as returning 69,895.62 against 69,895.611 and called it "12 ms out". **THE AGREEMENT IS REAL BUT IT IS NOT A SAME-INSTANT COMPARISON, and the 12 ms figure is finer than the inputs resolve.** The 69,895.62 is obtained at Eeloo's SOI radius; the frame that printed 69,895.611 sits at r 116,291,372 m, 2.79e6 m INSIDE the SOI, where the same model returns **68,240.923 s** -- 1,654.688 s / 2.37% away. The gap is not model error: it is the model's own SOI-radius -> frame-radius fall time, 1,654.699 s, matching to 0.011 s, which says the printed `ttPe` is referenced to the SOI-CROSSING instant and not to the 100,000x rails poll that straddled it (previous poll ut 46,920,150.399, this one 46,968,150.399). **THE LOAD-BEARING VALIDATION IS THE PERIAPSIS EPOCH INSTEAD, and it carries no interpretive step**: run the model from the PLAN-CAPTURE frame's own radius (ut 47,019,141.933, r 31,459,602.931 m) and it puts periapsis at ut 47,036,391.346 against the **ut 47,036,392.324** where MechJeb independently placed the capture node (`capture plan issued (circularize at periapsis ...)`) -- **0.978 s / 0.0057% out**, with the same ~1 s systematic reproduced from the first in-SOI frame, i.e. it lives in the printed elements rather than the propagation. Not circular: the SOI radius that would make the coast print exactly 69,895.611 is 119,082,920.9 m against Eeloo's published 119,082,940 m, 19.1 m apart, so the constant plugged in blind lands there untuned. Quote the coast agreement as within the **+/-0.04 s the 3-dp printed `ecc` resolves**, never as 12 ms. The header's 52,522 s row is the WRONG comparand: it is the perihelion-arrival, 1,000-km-park geometry, and this flight arrived at an 11,553 km periapsis. Do NOT read the 50,992 s SOI-entry -> CAPTURE-BURN-arm span against it for a tidy 0.971 -- those are different endpoints on different geometries, and the periapsis-epoch agreement above (0.978 s against MechJeb's capture node) is the real result. The Dres cross-check the header flags (20,869.7 s derived against the 20,689 an earlier draft printed, 0.87% apart) still does not reproduce and is NOT closed by this. CLAMP KEYS, MEASURED: `soiLeadSeconds` 100,000 is **1.43x the WHOLE 69,896 s approach**, so at Eeloo the clamp arms before SOI entry and stays armed throughout; `approachMaxWarpFactor` 5's one-frame-under-a-fifth rule measured **28.0x** margin (500 game s per poll against 13,979 allowed) rather than the 10.5x derived. ROUND 1 FIRED EARLY, AS DESIGNED: PLAN-CORRECTION entered 80,750.0 game s after COAST-TO-TARGET entry -- 0.19% of the coast -- of which ~27,700 s was the residual Kerbin-SOI escape (first heliocentric frame ut 5,124,183.6, round 1 planned 53,023 s later). At B19's 20e6 the trigger `dt = tts - threshold` stays positive until tts falls to 20e6, i.e. 21.87e6 game s in = **52% of the coast**, confirming the raise to 50e6. **THE HONEST COUNTERWEIGHT: THE CORRECTION CAP NEVER BOUND, AND IT IS NOT WHAT SAVED THIS FLIGHT.** Both rounds logged `classified fly (cap 550.0, floor 0.5); plan accepted` at **166.82** and **6.32 m/s** -- 30.3% and 1.1% of the cap -- with `extraRounds=0`, no arrival-quality extra granted, and `lf=1,305.522` at CAPTURE-BURN entry, 1.99x even the worst per-geometry capture floor (656 units). The four-round-at-cap tail the header derives sized for geometries this flight did not visit; the tails remain real and the asymmetry argument stands (a cap below the needed burn silently discards the node), but what made this flight were the two GAME-SECOND budgets, not the cap. **RISK 2 DID NOT FIRE, AND THE MARGIN IS THE FINDING**: lf ran 2,240.000 at TRANSFER-BURN entry (full LV-N load -- the ascent spends zero nuclear propellant, re-confirming B18/B19) -> 1,364.701 after ejection -> 1,305.522 at capture entry (934.478 spent, well inside the 1,440-unit radial group) -> **800.452 at ORBIT-COMMIT, i.e. 1,439.548 of the 1,440 spent**. The four radial drop tanks retained **0.452 units, 0.03% of the group**; `_2003` retained 1.135. So the 5-recording topology is measured and correct for both green runs AND is 0.03% away from being a 6-recording one. **READ THE RE-PINNED {5,5} AS A KNIFE EDGE, NOT A COMFORTABLE MEASUREMENT.** The concrete consequence: a marginally different arrival geometry, a slightly larger correction round, or any change to the craft or the park altitude spends those 0.452 units, autostage sheds istg 1, and the count becomes 6 -- **which would red the {5,5} pin on an entirely CORRECT flight**. When it happens, re-pin to the measured value and record which recording appeared (its span UT will fall inside a correction or the capture burn); do NOT widen the window back to a range, because B19's contract is pin the measurement and a range would re-hide exactly this transition. It also moves the flameout analysis: the two-trip reading assumes istg 1 is still attached, so on a flight that DOES shed, istg 1 is gone and a flameout pop lands on istg 0 (the pod decoupler) on the FIRST trip rather than the second. **ONE DERIVATION THE FLIGHT REFUTED**: the park came in at **11,351.2 x 11,334.7 km, ecc 0.000713** = 55.0 Eeloo radii, not the ~1,660 km / 7.9 radii the header predicted by carrying Dres's ratio across, so MechJeb's arrive-higher bias is far larger here than at Dres -- but 11,351 km would have cleared B19's own 20,000 km ceiling too, so the deliberate loosening to `parkMaxApoapsisMeters` 40,000 km was insurance this flight did not need (0.28x the new ceiling, 0.57x the old). OTHER MEASURED: jettison 2 pops, `avThr` 650,000 (Skipper) -> 60,000 (LV-N); ejection node ut 5,096,159.3 dv **2,119.457 m/s**; the ejection-window wait (TRANSFER-BURN span) 5,093,753.3 game s = 0.52x the 9,776,696 s Kerbin-Eeloo synodic and 3.54x inside the UNCHANGED 18e6 `transferBurnTimeoutSeconds`, confirming that key gains margin at Eeloo rather than losing it; capture burn 17,423.7 game s; commit ut **47,036,747.024** `terminalOrbitBody=Eeloo`; 20 phases, ending ORBIT-COMMITTED. WALL: 3,092 s against the 9,600 runtime / 9,000 mission budget = **0.32x** -- the budget was sized on the four-round-at-cap tail, which is a BOUND rather than an expectation, so this is not a reason to trim it, but the measured expectation for a green run is ~3,100 s. Recordings RE-PINNED **{5,5}** from {min=3,max=12} under B19's RE-PIN-do-not-widen contract (a KNIFE-EDGE pin -- see the 0.452-unit / 0.03% drop-tank margin above), every one accounted for by measured span UT: 20d890f9 main orbiter ut 26.2 -> 47,036,743.9 (Orbiting/Eeloo, 1,501 points) + c849bbee / d8bfbb2c ascent boosters ut 97.1 -> 149.0 / 148.7 (Destroyed) + 34ba13d3 autostaged core ut 1,697.3 -> 1,716.4 (Orbiting/KERBIN) + cf7ff6b8 jettisoned Skipper stack ut 2,699.0 -> 2,703.9 (Orbiting/KERBIN; the JETTISON PHASE itself ran 2,698.475 -> 2,703.315, ORBIT -> JETTISON -> PLAN-TRANSFER, so 2,703.9 is the RECORDING's end 0.6 s past the phase exit, not the phase exit) -- structurally identical to `dres-orbit-recorded` on every facet, same craft same profile | D1 auto-record-launch, commit-in-foreign-soi (EARNED: the `terminalOrbitBody=Eeloo` commit token); D3 orbital-checkpoint; D4 atmospheric/exo-propulsive/exo-ballistic/cohesive-cross-body-coast; D14 kerbin/eeloo/soi-count/warp-rails/warp-high/scene-flight/sandbox (EARNED: the `SOI change boundary suppressed in tree mode: Sun to Eeloo` token). | V12-eeloo-player-loop | operator (calibration discipline) | **FLOWN TWICE GREEN 2026-08-13 AND ARMED THE SAME DAY** -- V9's player-loop workflow pointed at the Eeloo program's committed recording (`eeloo-orbit-recorded`, B21's `2026-08-12_2239` output, tree `d4ce5c45adae4f3e89c4ac6bbec6b167`): kill the scene-entry stub, turn the three tracers on, mark the committed tree a LOOP unit, start loop playback, quit. Runs `2026-08-13_0053` (PASS attempt 1, wall 48 s) and `2026-08-13_0055` (PASS attempt 1, wall 49 s), BYTE-IDENTICAL on every decision token -- the two-consecutive-readings discipline satisfied. **HEADLINE 1 -- THE CADENCE MULTIPLE IS 5, the program's first reading DEEP in the span>synodic regime**: measured `cadence=48883481.632992938` against `synodic=9776696.3265985884`, and **cadence / synodic = 5.000000 EXACTLY** (equal to 5.0 in double; `cadence - 5*synodic` evaluates to 0.0, not to a rounding crumb). The law checks out on the RAW span rather than the compressed one: raw span = `compressedSpan` 41,950,304 + `cutSeconds` 5,086,416 = 47,036,720 (`spanDur=47036720.083578855` agrees), 47,036,720 / synodic = **4.8111**, `Ceiling(4.8111 - 1e-9) = 5` per `ReaimWindowPlanner.cs:144-146`, and `Math.Max(span, cadence)` keeps the ceiling because 5 x synodic exceeds the span by 1,846,761.5 s = 3.93%, the tightest cadence-over-span margin any committed unit has scheduled. THE THREE-TARGET REGIME TABLE now reads Eve 1.0733 -> 2, Dres 1.79 -> 2, **Eeloo 4.8111 -> 5** -- the first unit past 2. **HEADLINE 2 -- THE COMPRESSOR'S PURCHASE ON THE SPAN COLLAPSES AS THE COAST LENGTHENS, and this row previously overstated that as a regime inversion**: the compressor found ONE loiter and cut 5,086,416 s = **10.8%** of the span, with `tof=41843983.835473977` = **89.0%** of it. Dres cut 8,436,248 s out of 20,393,382 s = **41.4%**, against a Dres tof of 11,885,901.8 s = **58.3%** -- so **Dres was tof-MAJORITY too and nothing flipped**; the earlier "wait-dominated vs tof-dominated, opposite regime" phrasing was inflation and is retracted. What is real is the CUT FRACTION falling 3.83x (41.4% -> 10.8%) while the raw span grows 2.31x (20.39M -> 47.04M s), Eeloo's cut being SMALLER in absolute seconds on a span more than twice as long. Both spans are essentially wait + tof (Dres 99.65%, Eeloo 99.77%), which cross-checks that the compressor finds the whole ejection wait and nothing else: B21 measured its own TRANSFER-BURN span at 5,093,753 game s, so the compressor landed 7,337 s inside the wait it was meant to find. Same mechanism, same job done, far less of the span to take. OTHER MEASURED: `ENGAGED re-aim Kerbin->Eeloo via Sun`, `member#1 segs=20 startBody=Kerbin supported=True target=Eeloo`, `gatheredSegs=20 transferMemberSegs=20 plan.Supported=True`, `members=2 foreignMembers=0 overlaps=no`, `D0=54007648.196326263`, `anchor=48883507.852992937`, `phaseAnchor=53969923.596983388`. Those forbids are legitimate here only now: on an unmeasured fixture a decline is a legitimate READING, but this one has been measured twice and reads ENGAGED, so a decline is henceforth a regression. Unlike V9 this lane measured NO defect -- the cohesion fix V9 earned was already in the DLL and Eeloo exercises it from cold at interplanetary scale -- so the armed set is a regression floor for a HEALTHY reading rather than the inverse of a bug. **RECORDINGS RE-PINNED {6,6}** from the interim {3,14}, and the +1 is IDENTIFIED rather than inferred: on both runs the load-time optimizer split main recording `20d890f9f34b466fb8be07d83103a9a7` at `splitUT = 224.5398880005275` (PersistedPhaseChange, sec=4, Atmospheric -> ExoBallistic -- the ordinary ascent boundary, the same +1 V6M/V8/V9 record) into 'surface' [26.220 .. 224.540] 227 pts / 4 sections + 'exo' [224.540 .. 47,036,743.944] 1,275 pts / 54 sections, `Optimization pass: split 1 recording(s)`, with the exo half's other candidate rule-3 suppressed (`exoCoastBodyChangeKept=2`) -- V9's cohesion fix holding at Eeloo scale. The exo half's recording id is freshly generated per run (`80544d95...` on `_0053`, `f70cbb5c...` on `_0055`) and is NOT pinnable; the split UT, the two point counts and the resulting total are what reproduce. DO NOT WIDEN: a 7 means a second split appeared (most likely the cohesion regressing at the Kerbin->Sun handoff), a 5 means the ascent split stopped, an 8 means a scene-entry promotion survived the kill triple. saveParse identical on both runs: recordings 6, trees 1, committedTrees 1, terminalStates {Orbiting 3, Destroyed 2}, branchPoints {JointBreak 3}, points total 1,685 (the fixture's 1,684 plus the duplicated split boundary) largest 1,275 smallest 21 unparsed 0, supersede/tombstone/RP rows all 0. | V12A-eeloo-loop-arrival | operator (calibration discipline) | **BOTH PASSES FLOWN, ARMED AND NEGATIVE-CONTROLLED 2026-08-13** -- the Eeloo program's MEASUREMENT half: V10's TimeJump shape on the committed `eeloo-orbit-recorded` fixture (tree `d4ce5c45adae4f3e89c4ac6bbec6b167`), reaching the per-window `ReaimPlaybackResolver` synthesizer that V12 structurally cannot. Jumps 2/3/7 moved -16.804 / -8.886 / +1.597 s (the D0, tof and compressedSpan derivations were right, as V10's departure derivation was); jump 1 moved -7,337.403 s (the one-point Dres phaseAnchor calibration is good to ~1 part in 7,300 here, NOT the 0.17 s it looked like at Dres -- do not quote it as a law); **jumps 4/5/6 moved +61,000.032 s each, the arrival-bracket hypothesis failing outright rather than drifting.** The estimate modelled `soiEntryUT` as (D0+tof) minus one approach time, generalising Dres's measured 18,067 s centre-arrival -> boundary-crossing gap; **the product reports `soiEntryUT = D0 + tof` EXACTLY (95,851,632.03180024 both ways, to the digit) -- THERE IS NO DRES-STYLE GAP ON THIS LANE.** The Dres gap was real and is not refuted; it simply does not generalise. **HEADLINE 1 -- THE TILT IS A FINDING, NOT THE PREDICTION:** `tilt-correction inc-before=4.0725 bound=6.6500 targetInc=6.1500 incAch=NaN inc-after=4.0725 state=noop reason=in-plane`, byte-identical on four runs. `state=noop reason=in-plane`, NOT the predicted `retained`: the solved conic's 4.0725 deg is BELOW the 6.6500 bound, so `IsExcessiveTiltTransfer` is FALSE, control never enters the excessive branch (`incAch` NaN because it is only computed inside it) and nothing is touched. **EELOO TESTED THE BOUND ARITHMETIC, NOT THE RETENTION BRANCH** -- the retention branch lives INSIDE the excessive-tilt gate and the gate never opened, so at the highest inclination the arm has ever been aimed at (Eve 2.1 -> Dres 5.0 -> Eeloo 6.15) **the tilt plan's "Eve only ... Moho/Dres/Eeloo are unmeasured collateral" claim scope is NOT widened.** What would exercise it is a geometry whose SOLVED inclination exceeds the bound, not a higher-inclination TARGET (target inclination sets the bound, making the gate harder to trip); a 484-day near-Hohmann coast barely leaves the ecliptic, where Dres's shorter bent transfer reached `inc-before=13.1958`. Filed REAIM-TILT-NOOP-AT-EELOO-6.15-DEG. **HEADLINE 2 -- THE M-MIS-3 CLAIM IS NARROWER THAN THE PRE-FLIGHT HEADER ASSERTED, and the narrowing is the finding:** `eTarget=0.2600 halfWidthFraction=0.1900` proves the band law COMPUTES correctly at e=0.26 (clamp(0.06 + 0.5 x 0.26, 0.06, 0.20) = 0.19 exactly, the 0.20 cap NOT engaged -- it needs EccGain >= 0.5385 and the shipped 0.5 sits 7.7% below), but it does NOT prove the expansion BEHAVES. `ReaimTofSearch.BuildCandidateTofs` is RECORDED-centered: step 0 is always the recorded tof, the band is recordedTof +- k*step, and geomTof only picks `towardSign` -- so `devFromGeom=7577876.6` exceeding 0.19 x geom is IRRELEVANT (the band is never measured against geom), and `devFromRecorded=0s` means **cycle 0 was accepted AT STEP 0, so the expansion band was never walked.** Filed M-MIS-3-BAND-COMPUTED-NOT-EXERCISED; The reading run's own line kills it: `ExtractConstraints: ... members=1 launchBody=Duna ut0=9160398.0636915881 support=Supported constraints=1 [Orbital(Ike) same-parent P=65517.862134808071 off=17082.834318690002]` - **`constraints=1`, no Rotation(Duna) at all**, because an ORBIT-ROOTED recording has no surface phase and that constraint is emitted for a pad/surface-rooted launch. The collapse branch never gets a second constraint, so Tier 1 takes the single-constraint road: `PhaseLock APPLIED: ... anchor 9181612.89109542->9225915.9258263968 P=65517.862134808071 method=single-orbital cadence 21212.067403893918->65517.862134808071 fixedCadenceResidual=0 fixedCadenceWithinTol=yes zeroDrift=no`. Both OUTCOME predictions held exactly (`zeroDrift=no`, cadence exactly one P) and only the MECHANISM moved - which is why the armed pin is the whole conjunction on ONE line rather than `zeroDrift=no` alone, a token that reads identically under either road. THE GENERAL FACT THIS BUYS: phase-lock for a single-moon ORBIT-ROOTED subject is EXACT by construction - one constraint, residual literally 0, cadence = one moon period, no schedule - and the equality-band collapse is reachable only from PAD-ROOTED same-parent subjects. Propagated to `docs/dev/research/same-parent-reaim-jool-system.md` sections 3.5 / 6 / 7. The ~15.4 km periapsis drop is FINITE BURN - 937 m/s at 60 kN on 11.1 t is a = 5.40 m/s^2 and a 163.5 s burn straddling a periapsis crossed at 2,794 m/s - and it is SYSTEMATIC: **15,382 / 15,385 / 15,415 m across the three flights, a 33 m spread.** There is no periapsis-raising trim to reach for either: mlib's only round-out step (`_b5_park_trim_step`, circularize-at-APOAPSIS, armed by `parkTrimEccMax`) is called from `B5_CIRCULARIZE` only, which `startInOrbit = true` never enters. **THE FIX WAS `parkMinPeriapsisMeters` 60,000 -> 52,000 AND NOTHING ELSE** - the B17 precedent verbatim, nothing relaxed and an entry window resized to a measured delivery - and it is now vindicated in flight: across the three deliveries this profile has produced (55,089 / 56,240 / 56,667 m) the tightest sits 3,089 m above the floor and all three clear Laythe's 50 km atmosphere by 5.1-6.7 km, while the OLD floor would have refused every one. ### Committed, not yet green (1)

DOWN TO ONE ON 2026-08-20: the whole B26/V17 LAYTHE->VALL TRIO went green the same
day it became flyable - B26 on flight 3, then V17M (four reading runs) and V17T
(two) each completed the reading -> armed -> negative-control discipline. All
three rows live in the live-proven table above.
`B26-laythe-vall-transfer` flew on 2026-08-19 and both attempts refused in
PLAN-TRANSFER on a MECHJEB limitation - `OperationInterplanetaryTransfer.MakeNodes`
throws on a moon-parked origin - which BLOCKED the trio outright, filed as
`docs/dev/todo-and-known-bugs.md -> MECHJEB-INTERPLANETARY-PLANNER-REJECTS-MOON-ORIGIN`.
**THAT BLOCK IS LIFTED AS OF 2026-08-20: THE CAPABILITY IS BUILT, B26 IS RE-POINTED
AT IT, AND IT HAS NOW FLOWN ONCE.** mlib gained the flag-gated PARENT-RELAY mode (`parentRelayTransfer`) - candidate
(a)-minimal of the two the todo entry named: a new `ESCAPE` phase whose prograde node mlib
computes itself and kRPC's own `add_node` places (no MechJeb planner, which is why the
refusal cannot recur), flown by the existing NodeExecutor, then MechJeb's MOON-path
`OperationTransfer` planned from JOOL's frame once the escape has put the craft there.
**B26 FLIGHT 2 (2026-08-20) FLEW IT AND THE MODE'S CORE WORKED**, then measured TWO
defects of its own: (A) the escape was sized for a hyperbolic excess AT INFINITY when KSP
hands over AT THE SOI BOUNDARY, delivering 3.12x the intended departure speed; (B) the
coast's warp target was derived from a `time_to_soi` that flapped between two candidate
encounters, so the policy spent the phase cancelling its own warp. **FLIGHT 3 (run
`2026-08-20_1752`, PASS attempt 1) FLEW BOTH FIXES GREEN END TO END** - the full chain
through ORBIT-COMMITTED, a Vall park at ecc 0.0035, all four pre-registered observation
targets landing where the fixes predicted - and its produced save is harvested as
`fixtures/saves/vall-transfer-recorded`. B26 has moved to Live-proven; the todo entry is
CLOSED (the MechJeb limitation is ROUTED AROUND, not repaired). The V17 pair has since
completed its readings and its arming discipline and sits in Live-proven too. Read the original block correctly: it stopped the harness
PRODUCING the M-MIS-7 subject; it said nothing about the product question the trio was
built to ask, and the mode does not answer that question either. It is the THIRD two-stage program in three days and the
FIRST MOON-TO-MOON lane - `B26-laythe-vall-transfer` produces a recording rooted at
LAYTHE and targeting VALL, which `IsSameParentTarget` classifies CROSS-PARENT, and
`V17M` / `V17T` then measure what that does at `MissionConfig loop=true`.

WHY IT IS WORTH A PROGRAM. `docs/dev/research/same-parent-reaim-jool-system.md`
section 5.1 walks `TrySynthesizeTransfer` guard by guard for exactly this shape and
concludes the synthesizer "already works in a Jool-centric frame for P3 today,
unmodified" - which it calls "the single most surprising finding in this
investigation: Parsek may already be re-aiming moon-to-moon hops. It is untested,
unmeasured, and unrepresented in any harness lane." This trio is the lane that
tests it. Section 5.2 supplies the counter-argument that makes the reading
non-trivial: re-aim splices a centre-to-centre Lambert onto recorded legs that end
at the SOI SHELL, so the seam is of order one SOI radius - 0.23% of the orbit at
Duna, but **13.70% at Laythe and 5.58% at Vall**. Both outcomes are pre-registered
on V17M with the exact discriminating lines, and neither is gated.

TWO THINGS ABOUT THIS PROGRAM'S METHOD ARE NEW. First, **the machine side is a
COMPOSITION of two live-proven blocks that had never met** - the interplanetary
transfer path (eight lanes, always Kerbin-from-the-Sun) with Jool as the transfer
frame, entered through the orbit-start door (three lanes, always on the moon path) -
and it was audited end to end before a line was written, verdict CLEAN/values-only.
The audit paid for itself immediately: B7's `ejectionEccFloor = 1.05` WOULD NEVER
HAVE BEEN REACHED, because the required Laythe ejection hyperbola has ecc 1.0352.
Second, **the V17 seeds are weaker than any prior pair's and the spec says so**: the
anchor formula that served V14/V15/V16 is a phase-lock artifact, so a lane whose
routing is unknown cannot derive its brackets, and run 1 is explicitly a routing read
first and a calibration second.

UP TO FOUR AND EMPTIED THE SAME DAY: the B25/V16 LAYTHE TRIO landed on 2026-08-19
and all three flew that evening, leaving only the long-standing V3C holdout behind.
`B25-laythe-orbit` went first, then its produced save was harvested into the committed
`laythe-orbit-recorded` fixture, the loop pair was CALIBRATED off those bytes (tree
`0ffee6458331466481f5c7aa0212b515`, phaseAnchorUt 28,846,938.309, seam offset
1,027,136.107, cadence = TWENTY Laythe periods), and `V16M-laythe-player-loop` /
`V16T-laythe-ts-arrival` then flew their reading runs (`_2114` PASS, `_2115`
PARSEK-FAIL(anomaly) with all sixteen steps green - the catch that lane pre-registered)
and were ARMED off their own bytes. Four flights, one program, one day; all three rows
are in Live-proven above.

WHAT THE PROGRAM DELIVERED, in one line each. **k = 20 CONFIRMED LIVE** - the suite's
first loop cadence that is not one moon period, so the "whole multiple of the moon's
period" half of the research doc's section-3.5 claim finally had something to test.
**THE FIRST k > 1 RECURRENCE MEASUREMENT** - `seam-endpoint summary evaluated=2
outsideSoi=0` at BOTH cycles, 11.502 wall-seconds apart, which is research doc section
9.3's RECOURSE 1 proven and its "unmeasurable at seam pacing" limit retired for any lane
carrying the forty-tick dwell block. **AND A NEW REPORT-ONLY FINDING FAMILY** - the
creation-frame loop-shift binding gap, now visible on a SECOND lens because a k = 20
cadence puts the un-shifted epoch 1.06 Ms off the live clock; its `outsideSoi=1` reads
exactly like the Bop/Pol drift the research doc predicts and is NOT that, which V16M's
stepped-epoch zeros are what settles.

ORIGINAL SECTION NOTE FOLLOWS. `B25-laythe-orbit` flew out of this section that evening. It took TWO
flights - the first (`_1948` / `_2001`) INVALID(driver-flake) on its park WINDOW rather
than on the flight, having flown Jool -> Laythe inward clean through capture and
delivered a healthy 86,843 x 55,089 m Laythe park that its own declared floor refused;
the floor was resized to that measurement and the second (`_2039`) green'd attempt 1
through the full chain to ORBIT-COMMITTED. Its row is in Live-proven above. The V16 pair
stays here: it is now CALIBRATED off the harvested `laythe-orbit-recorded` bytes but has
not flown.

ONE THING FROM THAT PROGRAM IS WORTH CARRYING AS METHOD RATHER THAN RESULT. The V16
pair's jump table went through THREE states: authored from B25's planned profile it was
14,769 s off against a +-180 s bracket, i.e. the cycle-1 bracket would have missed the
arrival entirely; B25 FLIGHT 1 - a FAILED run, but one whose CLOCK was real - corrected
it to within 1.2 s BEFORE the fixture existed; and the harvest closed the last second.
That is the first time this suite has calibrated a downstream lane off an upstream
lane's failed run, and it is the strongest argument yet for the `PENDING_FIXTURE_LANES`
exemption that let the pair be committed early at all (now retired for the second
time). It is the SECOND two-stage program of its
kind - `B25-laythe-orbit` first (a first-flight reading lane whose produced save is
intended as the `laythe-orbit-recorded` fixture), then `V16M-laythe-player-loop` and
`V16T-laythe-ts-arrival`, both committed AHEAD of that fixture with a zero placeholder
tree id and calibration-seed jump UTs, tracked by the self-retiring
`SharedShipsManifestTests.PENDING_FIXTURE_LANES` exemption. That map went empty the day
the V15 pair retired its first two entries and the mechanism was KEPT rather than
deleted "because the shape recurs"; it recurred inside twenty-four hours, which is the
cheapest vindication that decision could have got.

WHY LAYTHE, AND WHY IT IS NOT "A FIFTH MOON". Two reasons, one about the research
programme and one about the machine.

- **It starts populating the J-table.** `docs/dev/research/same-parent-reaim-jool-system.md`
  section 3.1 carries a committed five-row table of the Jool moons and reasons over it
  for the whole of sections 3.3 / 3.4 / 6 - the 1:2:4 resonance, the Vall-anchored
  lattice, "adding Bop makes the required hold 7.4x longer than the window it must fit
  in", "adding Pol has no k in 4,096" - and NOT ONE ROW HAS EVER BEEN FLOWN. Section 9
  measured the arithmetic at GILLY, an *Eve* moon carried in that table only as the
  calibration case for the Bop/Pol rows. Laythe is the first Jool row itself, and it is
  first on purpose: at tol = SOI/v_orb = 1,155.03 s and duty 2.18e-2 it is the LOOSEST
  row in the table, 34x looser than Gilly's, so it asks the ROUTING question where a
  residual cannot corrupt the reading, exactly as Gilly asked the TOLERANCE question
  where a residual could not hide.
- **It is the first INWARD transfer.** Every b5 moon-path flight to date (Kerbin->Mun,
  Kerbin->Minmus, Duna->Ike, Eve->Gilly) parks LOW and transfers UP. B25's park sits at
  590,325,784.59 m - 3.28x Pol's orbit, outside the whole moon system - so the ejection
  is retrograde and the transfer's PERIAPSIS is the intercept. The b5 machine was
  audited end to end against that: five of seven areas are direction-agnostic by
  construction (the MechJeb plan call, the whole coast/SOI/warp machinery, the window
  logic, the flyby, the capture, and the burn-stagnation watchdog, which compares both
  apsides), and exactly TWO encode "outward" - both in the same family, evidence keyed
  to an apsis RISING. Both are handled with VALUES and nothing in `mlib` was touched;
  the full audit is in B25's spec header and summarised in its row below. One of the
  two, the mid-coast correction round, is a KNOWN ACCEPTED DEGRADATION rather than a
  fix, and the spec says so rather than papering over it.

AND ONE THING THE V16 PAIR ADDS THAT NO PRIOR LOOP LANE COULD: the FIRST k > 1 CADENCE,
plus the census-pacing instrument that makes it readable. Research section 9.3 named the
multi-cycle recurrence gap as its most consequential result - every phase-lock statement
the programme can currently make is a **k = 1** statement, because every loop subject so
far had a span below one moon period AND because both census lenses are
`VerboseRateLimited` on one per-class key that a fast-stepped two-cycle lane spends
once. B25's span is ~19.7 Laythe periods, so the cadence quantizes to k >= 20 and cycle 2
lands twenty moon orbits later; V16M's forty-tick `RecordingState` dwell block implements
that section's RECOURSE 1 verbatim so the cycle-2 summary escapes the 5.0 s window. Both
halves are needed and neither measures anything new alone.

WHY GILLY RATHER THAN A FOURTH CIRCULAR MOON. All three proven same-parent subjects
(Mun, Minmus, Ike) are near-circular, near-equatorial and have SOIs that are a large
fraction of their own orbits, so the phase-lock tolerance is thousands of seconds wide
and any residual disappears into it. Gilly is e = 0.55, i = 12 deg, SOI 126,123 m =
0.40% of its orbital radius, giving tol = SOI/v_orb = 247.6 s at the circular speed and
133-460 s across the eccentricity swing - the tightest duty cycle in the stock system,
tighter than Pol's, and 13.8x tighter than Ike's. The V15 pair is therefore the first
place a phase-lock residual would be MEASURABLE, and the miniature flyable version of
the Bop/Pol question in `docs/dev/research/same-parent-reaim-jool-system.md`.

WHAT THE PRE-REGISTRATION DISCIPLINE BOUGHT ON THIS PROGRAM, kept here because it is
the program's method rather than any one lane's result. The V15 pair was COMMITTED
BEFORE ITS SUBJECT EXISTED - placeholder tree id, calibration-seed jump UTs, and a
`saveTemplate` naming a fixture that did not exist, tracked by a self-retiring
exemption (`SharedShipsManifestTests.PENDING_FIXTURE_LANES`) that REDS the moment the
fixture appears. It did exactly that when B24 was harvested, and both entries were
removed the same day (the map is empty again, its healthy state; the mechanism is kept
for the next two-stage program). Writing the predictions down first is what makes them
scoreable, and THREE were scored: the ROUTING prediction HELD (single-orbital at a
second parent), the WATCH prediction HELD and inverted every prior loop lane (a genuine
entry at 775 m), and the TS-CONIC prediction was WRONG BY THREE ORDERS OF MAGNITUDE
(predicted sma ~ -48,900 m / ecc ~ 1.47; measured sma = -19.884 m / ecc = 1996.24,
because Gilly's mu is small enough that every approach hyperbola about it has |a| of
tens of metres). V15T additionally shipped with `allowedAnomalies = []` ON PURPOSE so
its sweep would MEASURE rather than swallow the parent-independence question - it red,
as pre-registered, and that red is what upgraded
MAPRENDER-ICON-OFF-ORBIT-CREATION-FRAME-AFTER-JUMP from a Duna/Ike observation to a
measured parent-independent trigger.

WHAT B24 LEFT BEHIND FOR THE PAIR, now measured rather than derived: the intercept
landed at a 24,903,858 m radius where Gilly moves at 630.0 m/s, so the pair's tolerance
is tol = SOI/v_orb = **200.2 s** - tighter than the 247.6 s circular figure, because
the encounter fell on the fast half of an e = 0.55 orbit, and 17.1x tighter than Ike's
~3,420 s. The recording is 115,360.886 s long of which the DESTINATION PHASE IS 381.490
s (92% of it is one 106,638 s transfer coast), which is what bounds where a park epoch
can sit and is a property of Gilly's 126,123 m SOI rather than of the flight. The
sharpened question the pair now asks: faithful playback shifts the replayed SOI-entry
epoch by exactly `k*P`, so `seam-endpoint summary ... outsideSoi=` read at CYCLE 1
against CYCLE 2 is the direct instrument - equal zeros mean the lock is exact at this
tolerance, a zero then a nonzero means a residual amplified linearly in `k`, which is
the eccentric-moon failure mode the Jool research doc predicts for Bop/Pol. Its fixture
`eve-park-kerbalx` was the suite's SECOND Parsek-stripped derived copy and the first
that had to strip crew bookkeeping (`KERBAL_SLOTS` / `CREW_REPLACEMENTS`) alongside the
B23 three - the subject is CREWED, which also makes it the first crewed same-parent
loop subject.

TWO LESSONS IT LEAVES BEHIND, because they are what the red flight bought and they
outlive the row. (1) **A RED RUN IS NOT A MEASUREMENT.** Both reds were PRODUCT defects,
not fixture shortfalls - the S1 plume-magnitude write threw `Object of type
'System.Single' cannot be converted to type 'System.Int32'` on every engine and RCS
emitter (`KSPParticleEmitter.minEmission`/`maxEmission` are int and `FieldInfo.SetValue`
does not convert), so the whole of S1 was a live no-op; and the sun-tracking pivot
measured its aim in the PARENT's frame while applying it in the PIVOT's, which on
`solarPanelOX10C` collapsed the zero-angle reference onto the axis and declined every
frame. The interim pin stayed deliberately UNCHANGED through that flight and was retired
only by the green one. (2) **A CELL THAT ASSERTS "X SURVIVES Y" MUST FIRST ASSERT X IS
NOT THE IDENTITY.** The RCS suppress/restore cell PASSED VACUOUSLY through the first
defect - a round trip over an unscaled baseline is a perfect round trip - while 66
headless cells pinned the ratio arithmetic without ever meeting the real field types. The
re-fly's `baselineEmission=400 scaledEmission=104 afterEmission=104` is that hole closed:
the cell now asserts the scaled value DIFFERS before asserting the round trip preserves
it, and the headless cells now reflect over the real `KSPParticleEmitter` type.

that is a committed tree's own launch (flight 1 was PASS attempt 1 and produced the
wrong subject anyway; no verifier in the chain could see it), which is filed as

why the fixture chain runs through a Parsek-stripped intermediate.

WHAT THE V14 PAIR WAS FOR, AND WHAT IT MEASURED. Both lanes flew their iteration-1
reading runs on 2026-08-18, were ARMED the same day off their own bytes, and completed
the discipline on 2026-08-19: armed re-flights `_0001` (V14M) and `_0002` (V14T), both
PASS attempt 1, plus ONE negative control `_0003` across the pair (a temporary
`supersedeRows = { min = 1 }` on V14M, correctly PARSEK-FAIL(save-structure), reverted).
Their rows are in Live-proven above; the account below is kept here because it is the
program's finding rather than either lane's.

THE PREDICTION THEY WERE BUILT ON WAS REFUTED, AND THAT IS THE PAIR'S VALUE. The
prediction was a THIRD routing outcome - `method=tidal-collapse` - on the grounds that
Rotation(Duna) and Orbital(Ike) are equal to 3 ms, 22x inside MissionPeriodicity's
equality band. Both runs answer `constraints=1`: there is NO Rotation(Duna) at all,
because an ORBIT-ROOTED recording has no surface phase and that constraint is emitted
for a pad-rooted launch. So the collapse branch never gets a second constraint and Tier
1 takes `method=single-orbital` instead. Both OUTCOME predictions held exactly
(`zeroDrift=no`, cadence exactly one Ike period) and only the mechanism moved - which
is why the armed pin is the whole conjunction on one line rather than `zeroDrift=no`
alone, a token that reads identically under either road.

WHAT IT BUYS BEYOND THIS PAIR: phase-lock for a single-moon ORBIT-ROOTED subject is
EXACT by construction - one constraint, residual literally 0, cadence = one moon
period, no schedule needed - and the equality-band collapse is reachable only from
PAD-ROOTED same-parent subjects. That is now propagated into
`docs/dev/research/same-parent-reaim-jool-system.md`, whose Jool-park analysis rested
on the same shape.

TWO OTHER ANSWERS WORTH THE READING. The named observation target - would the LOAD-TIME
optimizer split the same-parent Duna->Ike crossing? - came back COHESIVE
(`exoCoastBodyChangeKept=1`), so the count is 1 and that token is now required. And
V14T's reading run RED on one Tier-C `icon-off-orbit` raised in FLIGHT on a ghost-proto
creation frame; its ARMED run then raised it AGAIN (`counts={'icon-off-orbit': 1}` on a
PASS), which turns a single observation into a deterministic property of the
single-large-TimeJump shape - with V14M, same fixture and same arrival UT through a
stepped bracket, silent on both of its runs as the control. Tolerated by name, filed
report-only, no product change proposed.

### The GS-2/GS-3 orbital-deploy lane, all three LIVE-PROVEN (3)

The three rows below landed together on 2026-08-05 as Lane B of the rewind
gameplay-scenario wave. They are a CHAIN and must be flown in order: the forge
produces the fixture BOTH scenarios consume, and GS-3's entire value is the
DIFFERENCE from GS-2's outcome on one changed variable, so a GS-3 run against an
unflown GS-2 baseline measures nothing.

**The first two links are now flown.** The forge stamped `gs2-orbital-stack`, and
GS-2 flew its full arming discipline the same day: reading run `2026-08-05_0853`
PASS, armed run `2026-08-05_0856` PASS (`gating=True armed=['rewind']
mismatches=[]`). Both read **`rewindPoints: 1`** out of the produced save - THE
REWINDPOINT SURVIVED, which is the exact inversion of GS-1's `rewindPoints: 0` and
the FIRST LIVE MEASUREMENT of the R5 focus gate. Between the two specs, both
branches of `IsReapEligible` are now measured rather than reasoned about.

GS-3 then flew too (`2026-08-05_0903`, PASS attempt 1) and MEASURED the
divergence: `rewindPoints 0` against GS-2's `1` on the same fixture and mission,
one changed step. **That finding was FIXED the same day** (`570960da1`, the two-half
recorder + classifier repair - option (1) of the design question GS-3 raised), and
the post-fix reading run `2026-08-05_1132` (PASS attempt 1) measured
`rewindPoints 1`: the glance no longer costs the player anything. GS-3's window is
now INVERTED to `{min = 1}` - GS-2's verbatim - and ARMED, so the spec that FOUND
the bug is now the REGRESSION GUARD for its fix. Awaiting the armed-confirm and
negative-control flights. See GS3-NUDGE-DROPS-UNFINISHED-FLIGHT in
todo-and-known-bugs.md.

**GS-3's MEASUREMENT INVERTED, because the defect was FIXED (2026-08-05).** The
fix was an implementation change, not a design amendment - S17 stands: a
`VesselSwitchContinuation` branch point is now walked THROUGH by both Unfinished
Flights gates, and a BG-member switch consumption now stamps a classified terminal
on the recording it closes (the switch-closed origin used to end terminal-less,
which is what actually hid it from the classifier). Mechanism and the review
hardening in GS3-NUDGE-DROPS-UNFINISHED-FLIGHT, todo-and-known-bugs.md.
**Every measured fact in the GS-3 row below is PRE-FIX and is retained as the
historical record of the bug**; the post-fix re-fly, the inverted `{min = 1}`
window and the arming are covered by the three-run note that follows.

NO NEW CRAFT was authored for this lane, and that is the design decision worth
recording. The committed docking `Kerbal X.craft` in `bdock-forge-base` ALREADY is
an attached two-controllable stack: cutting its part tree at the istg=2
`Decoupler.2` gives ABOVE = crewed mk1-3pod + dockingPort2 + the Skipper orbital
stage (28 parts, keeps the root and therefore the focus) and BELOW = the spent
Rockomax core carrying `probeStackLarge`, the RC-L01 Remote Guidance Unit (21 parts
after the ascent sheds the radial boosters). Both sides carry a ModuleCommand, so
firing that decoupler IN ORBIT is a multi-controllable split - and the RP-authoring
path has NO atmosphere / altitude / situation / packed gate anywhere on it (the only
environment check is `HighLogic.LoadedScene != FLIGHT`, RewindPointAuthor.cs:82), so
an orbital split authors an RP exactly as an atmospheric one does. The 28/21 split
and the released vessel's name (`Kerbal X Probe`) are MEASURED, by EVA-2 and S0.8, on
the fixture this same decoupler produced. Building an orbital two-probe craft by
construction (the `build_gs1_craft.py` route) would have had to be flown to orbit by
MechJeb on its first ever flight for zero new coverage.

| Test case | Tier | Parsek surface verified | Coverage cells |
|---|---|---|---|
| FORGE-gs2-orbital-stack | operator | FIXTURE-FORGE, not a coverage flight. The FIFTH forge and the SECOND orbital one. Boots the same committed `bdock-forge-base`, launches the same docking Kerbal X with the same two named crew, and flies the LIVE-PROVEN `forge_lko` ascent + circularize - then parks with the spent core STILL ATTACHED. The one new mechanism is a `parkAttached` parameter on `forge_lko` (default FALSE = FORGE-eva2-lko's behaviour byte for byte): true makes CIRCULARIZE hand straight to PARK with the same park entry actions and NO stage activation, so the stamped save holds ONE vessel instead of two. A PARAMETER rather than a sibling machine because exactly one phase EDGE differs. Harvest with `harvest_bdock_station.py --run-save bdock-forge-base --target-name gs2-orbital-stack --expect-situation ORBITING` (the fifth reuse of the parameterized harvester; still no new tool). The one risk eva2 does not carry - MechJeb autostage firing the payload decoupler if the core ran dry - is caught by a new `stackAttached` assertion row that latches on any CIRCULARIZE/PARK frame whose `vessel_count` rises above the CIRCULARIZE-entry baseline, and it errs SAFE (its one false-positive channel is a stock asteroid spawn, which costs one re-run; a bad fixture would cost every consumer) | D14 kerbin/scene-flight/sandbox. A forge asserts nothing about Parsek: `logContracts.required` is empty on purpose, so a failure is driver-INVALID and never a PARSEK-FAIL |
| GS-2-orbital-probe-deploy | operator (the reading run + arming sequence are DONE; the only step left is the cadence PROMOTION, a human call) | **THE FOCUS GATE, and the FIRST live exercise of it.** An attached two-controllable stack parked at ~100 km fires its decoupler IN ORBIT; both halves stay loaded and Orbiting; focus stays with the crewed pod stack. Expected per R5: the NON-FOCUS leaf qualifies (`reason=stableLeafUnconcluded`, UnfinishedFlightClassifier.cs:334) and is demoted to CommittedProvisional - an Unfinished Flight row - while the FOCUS leaf is refused by the focus-slot gate (`reason=stableTerminalFocusSlot`, :313-322), so with one slot open `IsReapEligible` declines and the RewindPoint SURVIVES. This is the EXACT INVERSION of GS-1's both-reap direction, which GS-1's own header explicitly disclaims as a separate lane, and it implements the never-written `InvertedFocusBoosterUpperStageOrbitingTest` of `parsek-rewind-to-separation-design.md` section 13.2 (scenario S17/S19b of `extending-rewind-to-stable-leaves.md` section 8). The positive half is Info and REQUIRED (`CommitTree promoted [chain-tip ]rec=... reason=stableLeafUnconcluded to CommittedProvisional`, RecordingStore.cs:1235/:1265). The non-reap is only available at VERBOSE - `IsReapEligible` is COMPLETELY SILENT when it refuses (RewindPointReaper.cs:184-222 emits no log call at any level), so the assertion is the caller's Verbose summary `ReapOrphanedRPs: reaped=0 remaining=[1-9]...` (:125) with `Reaped rp=` FORBIDDEN; `verboseLogging=true` is therefore MANDATORY here rather than the convenience it is in GS-1. The focus-slot verdict line itself is deliberately NOT required (VerboseRateLimited, and reachable only if `ApplyRewindProvisionalMergeStates` gets as far as calling `TryQualify` for the focus recording) - it is the single best arming candidate after the reading run. The ~100 km park is LOAD-BEARING: `DetermineTerminalState` demotes Orbiting to SubOrbital when the periapsis is inside the atmosphere, and the focus gate fires ONLY for Orbiting | D1 commit-scene-exit/auto-merge; D5 controlled-decoupled-child; **D9 rp-disk-reaper** (the REFUSAL branch, where GS-1 gates the REAP branch - between them both outcomes of `IsReapEligible` are pinned); D14 kerbin/sandbox/scene-flight/scene-ksc. `[expectations.rewind]` (`rewindPoints = { min = 1 }`, the inversion of GS-1's `max = 0`) is **ARMED** (`gating = true`) at HEAD after the reading run `0853` and the armed run `0856`, and the spec is in `ARMED_ALLOWLIST`; `[expectations.recordings.structure]` remains report-only. NOT claimed: no registry value exists for "an Unfinished Flight row was opened"; `unfinished-flights-stash` would be a false claim since nothing is stashed, so the gap is left visible |
| GS-3-switch-nudge-deployed | operator (flown 3x; ARMED as the S17 regression guard - the only step left is the cadence PROMOTION, a human call) | **THE S17 SWITCH NUDGE, and a DESIGN-VS-IMPLEMENTATION DIVERGENCE under test.** GS-2's flight plus ONE `SimulateStockSwitchClick` onto the released vessel - one changed variable, same fixture, same mission. `extending-rewind-to-stable-leaves.md` section 8 S17 says a nudged probe STAYS in Unfinished Flights ("v1 over-includes nudged probes; player handles via Seal"); the CODE says otherwise. `CreateSwitchContinuationSegment` writes `parentRec.ChildBranchPointId` onto the released leaf's ORIGIN recording (SwitchSegmentBuilder.cs:422-465), and `TryQualify`'s downstream-branch gate (UnfinishedFlightClassifier.cs:115-132) then returns `downstreamBp` FALSE before the terminal logic runs - so the leaf is NOT promoted, both slots stay Immutable, and the RP REAPS. If the flight confirms it, a player who merely LOOKED at a deployed probe silently loses its re-fly affordance, and that is a finding for `todo-and-known-bugs.md` with both run ids as evidence, not something to soften the scenario around. A NO-INPUT nudge suffices: the segment IS classified no-op, but its disposition is `BgMemberOrMixed` (the tree holds the pod stack's recordings too, RecordingStore.cs:3283-3286) and `TryAutoDiscardNoOpSwitchSegment` DEFERS that disposition (SceneExitInterceptor.cs:407-415) - only Standalone / CommittedRestoreClone discard - so the branch point persists into the commit. A meaningful throttle blip was deliberately NOT used: it would move the branch point AND the child's orbit, and a one-variable A/B must not change two. THERE IS NO SWITCH BACK: with a session armed and a different target, `DecidePreSwitchDialogAction` returns OpenDialog (Case A) and the v1 verb answers REJECTED `dialog-required case=A-session` without auto-answering the modal - so the second switch step is a deliberate `expect = "REJECTED"` probe pinning that contract **FLOWN 2026-08-05, run `2026-08-05_0903`, PASS attempt 1 - AND THE DIVERGENCE IS MEASURED.** saveParse: `rewindPoints 0`, `supersedeRows 0`, `tombstones 0`, `pointsTotal 453 largest 228 smallest 3` (the 3-point recording IS the persisted no-op switch segment - `BgMemberOrMixed` is deferred at scene exit, so a zero-input glance still outlives the commit). Against GS-2's `rewindPoints 1` on the same fixture and mission, one changed step: the probe's Unfinished Flight row and its RewindPoint both disappear. THE PREDICTED MECHANISM WAS WRONG and the measured one is sharper: `downstreamBp` never appears in the log at all. The switch segment DISPLACES the origin as the classifier's subject (`not promoted reason=noMatchingRP parentBp=<the VesselSwitchContinuation bp>`), while the probe's origin recording - the one the RP slot points at, terminal `Orbiting` - is never evaluated. Filed as GS3-NUDGE-DROPS-UNFINISHED-FLIGHT; the design call was taken the same day IN FAVOUR OF THE IMPLEMENTATION CHANGE (tolerate the inert switch bp; section 8 S17 stands), so **every measured fact in this cell is PRE-FIX and the expected reading inverts to `rewindPoints 1` on the next re-fly** - see the lane note above. STAYS REPORT-ONLY: arming `rewindPoints = {max = 0}` would pin the bug as the contract. | D1 switch-segment/commit-scene-exit/auto-merge; D5 controlled-decoupled-child. **D9 DELIBERATELY UNCLAIMED**: the reaper's behaviour here is the PREDICTION under test, carried report-only, and claiming coverage off a block that cannot red would be claiming it from nothing. NOT claimed either: D5 `chain-continuation-switch`, on S0.8's own ground - a switch segment attaches under a BRANCH POINT and `CreateSwitchContinuationSegment` writes no ChainId. All three divergence facts (`downstreamBp`, the ABSENCE of a promotion, the reap) are **REPORT-ONLY** on flight 1; the required set is only the mechanically-certain click/consume/refusal/commit tokens |

### In-game batch wiring: the M1 snapshot baseline, H32 (1), LIVE-PROVEN

The `SnapshotBaseline` category and its scenario were authored TOGETHER, for the M1
ghost snapshot-baseline fix (branch `ghost-snapshot-baseline`) - the first member of
this family that is not a wiring pass over a pre-existing category. It exists because
the fix has a surface no headless test can reach: the M1 parse and the pure action
resolution are covered by 74 xUnit cells, but every applier that MOVES something is
Unity-coupled, and calling `GhostPlaybackLogic.SetLightState` or a canopy applier from
xUnit throws `SecurityException` ("ECall methods must be packaged into a system
module") before it runs a line. Whether a snapshot saying `deployState=EXTENDED`
actually puts a ghost's transforms somewhere other than the prefab stow pose therefore
has no answer outside a live game.

Same H7-H20 shape: LoadGame, pin `autoRecordOnLaunch` false, one `RunTests` naming one
category, FlushAndQuit. Every cell authors its OWN one-part snapshot ConfigNode over a
stock part it discovers through `PartLoader`, so the spec injects no corpus and pins a
zero recordings count.

**LIVE-PROVEN 2026-08-11, run `2026-08-11_1111`, PASS attempt 1, wall 52 s, fully
unattended** - every verifier PASS (driverValidity, batchComplete found=True failed=0
perCategory=1, analyzer red=0, logValidate, anomalySweep `hits=[] counts={}`,
expectations mismatches=0), `unityExceptions` REPORT total=3 (NullReferenceException;
NOT armed off one reading), saveParse REPORT clean and the zero-recordings leak pin
held (`pointsTotal=0`, count 0).

THE INTERIM PIN IS RETIRED AND BOTH REASONS FOR IT TURNED OUT NOT TO HOLD, which is
worth more than the pin. The batch read `BATCH_COMPLETE v1 total=7 passed=7 failed=0
skipped=0 category=SnapshotBaseline scene=FLIGHT` - ALL SEVEN CELLS EXECUTED, nothing
skipped - so the spec now pins the tally whole and
`IngameBatchWiringGroupTests.INTERIM_PIN_IDS` is EMPTY (its healthy state). (1) The
claim that "one cell needs the Breaking Ground expansion the `stock-minimal` profile
does not ship" is FALSE: the Clone phase junctions the dev install's entire
`GameData/SquadExpansion` (`junction GameData/SquadExpansion -> ...`), which carries
`Serenity/Parts/Robotics/` - hinge_01/03/04, piston_01, the rotors - so Breaking Ground
IS present and `SnapshotServoAngle_PosesTheGhostServo` executed, logging `Snapshot
baseline applied: parts=1 deployables=0 parachutes=0 lights=0 servos=1
servosSkipped=0`. The end-to-end pose proof therefore LANDED rather than being deferred
to "a profile with the expansion" - though on THAT flight it landed over the wrong key
(the live probe plan's transient `currentAngle`, which no production snapshot carries);
the cell was rewritten onto the PERSISTED key and re-flown, run `2026-08-11_1643`, where
it passed over `hinge.01` / `targetAngle`. The expansion-presence finding is unaffected:
the re-fly's candidate is a Breaking Ground hinge too. (2) The four deployable cells' requirement was an honest build-time
measurement, and it came back positive - all four executed, each logging `Snapshot
baseline applied: parts=1 deployables=1 ...`, so no cell in this category is currently
vacuous or deferred. GENERAL LESSON, recorded at the removal site in `test_hlib.py`: a
future interim pin justified by "the profile lacks X" should CHECK THE PROVISIONED
INSTANCE rather than reason from the profile's name. The measured split also agrees
with the attribute derivation (`hlib.derive_batch_tally` -> total 7, attribute_skipped
0), so the spec needs no `RUNTIME_SKIPS` entry.

| Test case | Tier | Parsek surface verified | Coverage cells |
|---|---|---|---|
| H32-snapshot-baseline | nightly | The M1 ghost snapshot baseline against LIVE transforms: `deployState=EXTENDED` and gear `stateString=Deployed` pose the built ghost at its DEPLOYED transform poses, a mid-travel `EXTENDING` deliberately produces no baseline and leaves the pre-M1 stow pose, a non-zero servo pose read through `GhostVisualBuilder.TryResolveSnapshotRoboticPoseKey` - the key a real snapshot PERSISTS (hinge / rotation servo `targetAngle`, piston `targetExtension`), not the live probe plan's transient primary - moves the servo transform off its stowed pose (Breaking Ground only - skips naming the requirement otherwise), the loop-cycle `RestoreRoboticSpawnBaselines` returns a displaced servo transform to its spawn pose, and a MODULE-less snapshot PART produces no baseline dictionary at all. Positional assertions scale their tolerance through `InGameFixtureMath.SceneFloatGridToleranceMeters`; a candidate part qualifies only when its stowed and deployed poses are measurably separated, so a cell cannot pass over an animation that moves nothing | D14 sandbox/scene-flight. NOT claimed: any D6 playback token (nothing is positioned or played back) and no D16 sidecar token (the snapshots are authored in-memory). **LIVE-PROVEN 2026-08-11** (`2026-08-11_1111`, PASS attempt 1, wall 52 s): `total=7 passed=7 failed=0 skipped=0` - all seven cells EXECUTED, including the servo cell the header had predicted would skip (stock-minimal junctions SquadExpansion, so Breaking Ground robotics ARE present). Tally now pinned whole; `INTERIM_PIN_IDS` emptied. See the section note above for both falsified premises. **RE-PROVEN 2026-08-11 after the servo-key fix** (`2026-08-11_1643`, PASS attempt 1, wall 53 s, branch tip `aee854e03`): the pin printed token for token, all seven cells PASSED individually, and the rewritten servo cell EXECUTED over the persisted key - `'hinge.01' servo 'ModuleRoboticServoHinge' posed to 37.5 via persisted key 'targetAngle' at spawn (mode=Rotational)`, `servos=1 servosSkipped=0`. `unityExceptions total=0` on this run (3 on the first) |

### In-game batch wiring: the Logistics inter-body slice, H34 (1)

The first spec to own any part of `Logistics`, the largest in-game category in the
tree (47 declarations) and the one the inventory doc's B4 bucket names as the worst
"specs that all Skip" hazard. It does NOT wire the category: it wires the
SPACECENTER-eligible SLICE of it, which is where the one automated proof of the REAL
inter-body loop-unit shape lives. Deliberately partial, and the pin carries the
45-declaration skip floor in the open rather than hiding it behind a whole-category
claim.

ID NOTE: authored and FLOWN as `H32-logistics-inter-body`; renamed H34 on the merge
into main, where a sibling lane had already landed a different `H32-snapshot-baseline`
(the section above). The run ids in the row below embed the OLD name and remain true
history - nothing was re-flown and no pin moved, because `BATCH_COMPLETE` carries no
spec id and every pinned token is a production emission.

| Test case | Tier | Parsek surface verified | Coverage cells |
|---|---|---|---|
| H34-logistics-inter-body | operator | The M5 inter-body builder-shape gate driven end to end against LIVE stock ephemerides: a REAL committed Kerbin->Duna transfer tree, promoted to a Route, resolved through the REAL `RouteOrchestrator.ResolveLoopUnit` -> `RouteBackingMission.BuildMission` -> `MissionLoopUnitBuilder.TryBuildLoopUnitForSelection` with the live `FlightGlobalsBodyInfo.Instance`, then driven through the live firing gate. This is the ONLY automated coverage of the builder-produced unit shape: every M5 xUnit drives hand-built `LoopUnit` / `ReaimMissionPlan` / `ReaimWindowSchedule` structs, because re-aim engages only on the `bodyInfo != null` path and headless `FlightGlobals` is empty, so the singleton degrades to no-body-info and re-aim can never engage. The gated failure mode is the M5 review's exact one - a silent degrade to `FlatInterval` in which N is ignored, with no windowed label or countdown and nothing red anywhere. Also the second consumer of the R12 `LoadGame scene=` argument (after H23) and the first to use `scene=spacecenter`, on a fixture that would otherwise take the Focusable/FLIGHT route | D10 inter-body-nth-window + dispatch-cadence (both ZERO-DECLARER before this spec); D14 sandbox/scene-ksc. **LIVE-PROVEN 2026-08-11, three runs, all PASS attempt 1**: `2026-08-11_1549` (73 s) was a deliberately under-gated READING run - tally split unpinned as `passed=[1-9][0-9]* skipped=[0-9]+`, no route tokens - and `2026-08-11_1553` (50 s) / `2026-08-11_1554` (49 s) are consecutive confirms on the fully pinned shape. Tally MEASURED whole: `total=47 passed=2 failed=0 skipped=45 category=Logistics scene=SPACECENTER`, identical in all three collected logs. The informative half of the reading is the split: the 45 skips are ENTIRELY the attribute floor (45 FLIGHT-scoped declarations scene-skip; 0 batch-skipped among the eligible), and ZERO members self-skipped at run time even though the inter-body cell carries three live guards - a non-stock body pack, a live-classifier decline of the synthetic chain, and a loiter-cut / arrival-hold unit shape whose non-identity within-cycle phase would invalidate the firing-gate arithmetic. On stock geometry none fired. The two route tokens are PRODUCTION emissions from `RouteOrchestrator.ProcessLoopRoute`, not test echoes: the modulo-skip line carries `basis=ReaimWindows` AND `N=2` AND `nothing emitted` (so a Flat classification or a lost residual cadence cannot emit it), and the `cycle=cycle-0 FIRED full cycle (dispatch+debit+delivered)` line proves the alternation's other half actually delivers on the FIRST owed crossing, so a route that skipped every window also reds. The Verbose-level skip token is why the driver carries a `verboseLogging=true` step. Unity exceptions read 0 on all three runs and are deliberately left REPORT-only rather than armed at `maxTotal=0` - one clean reading is not a mechanism argument. TIER: `operator`, and promotion to `daily` is an OPEN HUMAN CALL - nothing technical blocks a cadence (three unattended PASSes), but putting the largest in-game category on the daily tier is a cadence decision for a human |

### In-game batch wiring: the Logistics route-proof slice, H35 (1)

The SECOND half of the `Logistics` split, and the one that needed a RECORDED fixture.
Where H34 wires the 2 SPACECENTER-eligible declarations, H35 wires the 8
FLIGHT-eligible ones; between them the two specs drive every Logistics declaration a
batch can execute at all, and the remaining 38 (`AllowBatchExecution = false`) stay
the inventory doc's B4 bucket. Five of the eight are the ROUTE-PROOF READ SIDE - pure
walkers over each recording's `ROUTE_CONNECTION_WINDOWS` node and its
`ROUTE_ORIGIN_PROOF` field, which `InGameAssert.Skip` when the loaded save carries
nothing to walk. Every earlier Logistics-touching run loaded a recording-free fixture,
so all five had always skipped and their proof-shape asserts had never executed
anywhere.

The lane's scope is deliberately the PROOF surface, not route CREATION. The fixture's
route-owning tree carries two `CommittedProvisional` recordings, so
`RouteCandidateFinder.IsTreeFullySealed` (every recording must be
`MergeState.Immutable`) fails and neither tree can become a route candidate; the
`SealSlot` seam verb that would seal one is RESERVED (`not-implemented-v1`). See
`ROUTE-CANDIDACY-GATED-ON-SEAL-NO-SEAM-PATH` in todo-and-known-bugs.md.

### In-game Rewind block, R7 (2)

`Rewind` was the largest undriven in-game category in the tree - 37 declarations,
more than any other - and two prior wiring waves left it alone for the reason the
inventory doc's bucket B4 gives: 24 of the 37 carry run-time `InGameAssert.Skip`
guards, so a spec that boots and skips 30 of them is legal and worthless.

**The category is BIMODAL, and that is what decided the shape.** Reading all 37
bodies plus every helper they reach: twelve members gate on
`scenario.ActiveReFlySessionMarker != null`, and four gate on it being NULL and
skip when one exists ("would clobber ownership tracking" / "would corrupt live
RenderSessionState") - four as written, and five once this branch's
`F5MidReFlyResume` foreign-session skip guard is counted. The two sets are
disjoint, so no single boot executes both.
A third group is SPACECENTER-scoped and unreachable from any FLIGHT batch. Hence
a spec per precondition mode rather than one spec per category.

Flight 2 (run `2026-07-30_2023`, wall 2,044 s, FULLY UNATTENDED): MISSION-OK, zero unmet rows, identical verifier picture and the IDENTICAL baseline `hitCounts={line-blink: 2}` `unlistedReasons=[]`. The sweep FAIL preempts the expectations verifier, so all 12 required pins + the forbidden-ERROR contract were evaluated MANUALLY against both collected logs (`logs/2026-07-30_2322`, `logs/2026-07-30_2357`): all 12 matched on both (130/130 nonzero-ghost probe frames, 12/12 `faithful-parity summary sampled=1 overTolerance=0` passes, zero `[Parsek][ERROR]`), so the faithful-parity `sampled>0` pin's stated measurement protocol is CLOSED - the lens measures real replayed geometry, and its 55-per-run `skip.reaimed-or-foreign-seed` passes are the injected-b9-corpus ghost, not the flown one. WHAT THE BASELINE SAYS FOR ARMING (known-gate 0): across three dwells (incl. the discarded first attempt) `line-blink` fired EXACTLY 2x per dwell on the flown MAIN recording's proto orbit line under the 100x ramp step, at ownership-handoff frames, and NOTHING else fired - zero unlisted reasons, zero icon-teleport/icon-off-orbit. So: (a) `line-blink` is deterministic on this geometry and is a REAL defect signal or a benign handoff transient - diagnose before arming (forensics: todo `V1-REPLAY-LINE-BLINK`); (b) a future budget of `{ token = "line-blink", maxCount = 2 }` would exactly fit the measured behavior IF the blink is ruled benign - do not arm it before the diagnosis; (c) the nine ungated reasons stayed SILENT on real geometry, which is the first actual evidence for the gate-vs-instrument call on them. PROMOTE TO a cadence only after the line-blink call is made (either fixed -> green runs, or ruled benign -> budgeted). An earlier same-day invocation burned two full flights on HARNESS-INJECT-FAILS-OPEN (fresh worktree; evidence appended to that todo entry). **LINE-BLINK CALL MADE 2026-08-01 (branch `fix-v1-line-blink`): verdict (B) BENIGN, in BOTH of the two dark windows per dwell but by TWO DIFFERENT mechanisms** - window A is the proto orbit line standing down while the trajectory polyline OWNS the same ghost's non-orbital leg (the anti-double-draw invariant working), window B is the head crossing BETWEEN recorded legs, where the conic is held down because the current orbit is a per-frame state-vector reseed (`e 0.9893 -> 0.9993 -> 0.9999 -> 0.8939` across eight frames) while four run legs and two forward arcs stay painted. The rails ramp compresses each into 3-8 frames, so two correct transitions fall inside `LineBlinkFrameWindow = 8`. Full forensics: todo `V1-REPLAY-LINE-BLINK`. Suppressed at the RAISE SITE with a discriminating guard, NOT with a budget: `MapRenderTrace.IsLineBlink` gained an `offWindowCovered` param (mirroring `bodyChanged`) fed by the pure `NextOffWindowUncovered`, so a dark window with an ACTUAL polyline line PAINTED on every frame is not a blink while a window with even one unpainted frame still raises. So this spec arms NO budget and adds NO `allowedAnomalies` entry - the arming guidance's benign-and-suppressed case. **MEASURED, not asserted: run `2026-08-01_1551`** (collected log `logs/2026-08-01_1925`, 2,047 s wall, FULLY UNATTENDED, attempt 1) flew the FIRST cut of the guard, which fed it the narrow current-leg OWNERSHIP bit, and read `hitCounts={line-blink: 1}` `unlistedReasons=[]` - it killed window A exactly as designed and left window B, whose survivor arrived stamped `offWindowCovered=False polylineOwns=False sinceFrames=8`. That stamp is what located window B: ownership answers "is the HEAD inside a drawn CURRENT leg" and goes false in inter-leg gaps where the trajectory is still painted, so the coverage input was switched to a new per-recording PAINTED signal (`GhostMapPresence.IsPolylinePaintingGhostTrajectory`, published by the same Driver decide walk at exec-order -50 from a current-element leg OR a forward run leg OR a forward arc). Still strictly ACTUAL-DRAW - the Director's TracedPath classification is never consulted - so the token still MEANS "the line went dark and nothing was painted", strictly sharper than the pre-guard signal; every raise carries `offWindowCovered=` / `polylinePainted=` / `polylineOwns=`, the last two together precisely because they disagree in the gaps. Everything else on that run was green (MISSION-OK, all rows met, 12 phases, driverValidity/analyzer/logValidate PASS, 130 nonzero-ghost probe frames of 399, 13 `faithful-parity sampled>0` with `overTolerance=0`, zero `[Parsek][ERROR]`, all 12 required pins matched manually since the sweep FAIL preempts the expectations verifier). **CONFIRMED GREEN 2026-08-02, run `2026-08-02_1046`: `hitCounts={}` `unlistedReasons=[]`, verdict PASS** - 2,082 s wall / 1,988 s mission, attempt 1, fully unattended, and V1's FIRST fully green run. Every verifier green INCLUDING `expectations`, which the earlier sweep FAILs had been preempting: the harness itself evaluated all 12 required pins + the forbidden-ERROR contract this time instead of a human doing it by hand. Non-vacuous on the same evidence as before (131 nonzero-ghost probe frames of 399, 13 `faithful-parity sampled>0` with `overTolerance=0`, zero `[Parsek][ERROR]`), and ZERO Tier-C raises of any reason - all nine ungated report-only reasons stayed silent too. The row is therefore GREEN-BY-RIGHTS. **RE-PROVEN AT HEAD 2026-08-02, run `2026-08-02_1336`** (2,046 s, attempt 1, unattended, `hitCounts={}`, all verifiers green, 130 nonzero-ghost frames, zero raises of any reason) after a four-reviewer pass reworked the paint signal - per-member attribution instead of a frame-wide counter delta, degenerate line reads no longer able to end a dark window, per-pid line history pruned on ghost teardown, and the decide-time-not-paint-time semantics stated honestly. That run also carries the new `line-blink-suppressed` line, so a green dwell now SHOWS its two by-design windows being caught (`offWindowCovered=True polylinePainted=True polylineOwns=False` at sinceFrames=3 and 8) instead of merely not raising - a silent guard on a gated token was itself a finding. **PROMOTED 2026-08-02: `tier = "operator"` -> `tier = "nightly"`**, on the spec's own stated terms - green flights with run ids, wall time and an anomaly baseline recorded here. Cost is measured, not estimated: ~2,055 s (~34 min) per UNATTENDED run across four of them (2,044 / 2,047 / 2,082 / 2,046 s; the earlier 1,598 s flight was operator-assisted and does not count). The ~4,900 s x retry = ~2.7 h ceiling still stands as the budget, but it is the systematic-failure case the operator tier existed to keep off a cadence, and two consecutive greens at the corrected guard are the evidence it is not the expected case. NOT daily (~34 min every day is disproportionate for a lane catching RARE real-geometry regressions) and NOT weekly (no spec uses that tier, so it would look scheduled while very likely never firing). Same chain as V6T on the Minmus axis, parked ~142 s past the cycle-1 Kerbin->Minmus SOI entry (predicted anchor 1,077,505.690 held exactly, so no re-pin). **FLOWN TWICE 2026-08-08, `2026-08-08_1614` and `2026-08-08_1616`, both PARSEK-FAIL(anomaly) attempt 1, wall 51 s each.** All 16 steps met on both, driverValidity/analyzer(red=0)/logValidate/expectations PASS, saveParse REPORT clean; the ONLY failing verifier is anomalySweep, `hits=['icon-off-orbit'] counts={'icon-off-orbit': 1}`. THE RAISE, and the line is unusually self-diagnosing (EXCERPTED: `recId=`, the second `effUT=`, `LAN=79.908` and `argPe=351.933` are elided for width, and `pid=`/`frame=` are PER-RUN - run `_1616` reads `pid=2354385572 frame=6778`; every field the finding rests on is byte-identical across the two flights): `phase=Anomaly surface=ProtoIcon pid=1830757804 frame=6791 currentUT=1345355.000 reason=icon-off-orbit angleIconVsOrbitEff=131.22 angleEffVsLive=0.00 loopShift=0.0 \| lonIcon=-57.59 lonOrbitEff=169.32 lonOrbitLive=169.32 \| iconR=2212724 orbitEffR=2212724 \| lineActive=True inc=7.304 sma=-32531 ecc=4.0269 body=Minmus`. Full cross-lane write-up: `MOON-LOOP-FINDINGS` in todo-and-known-bugs.md, which files this raise and V7M's end-inside-watch teardown NRE together as the quartet's two product observations. The effective and live orbits AGREE exactly (`angleEffVsLive=0.00`), the icon is at the correct RADIUS to the metre (`iconR == orbitEffR`), and it is 131 degrees stale ALONG that orbit - a stale along-track position on a correct conic, not a random placement. It fires in the FLIGHT half (frame 6791, ~2 s before the TS ghosts are created), so this is not a tracking-station defect. WHAT IT IS NOT: not "a large single jump" (V7M's first bracket jump is the same ~268 ks move and sweeps clean) and not "a single jump crossing the SOI seam" (V6T does exactly that on the Mun axis and sweeps clean). What is left is the conjunction plus destination geometry - this is the only run that lands, in ONE move, INSIDE a long high-eccentricity capture leg (Minmus ecc 4.03, occupied 6,092 s) rather than before the seam (V7M) or into Mun's ecc 1.67 leg transited in 44 s (V6T). CANDIDATES, NOT A CONCLUSION; the discriminating experiment is named in the spec header (fly a variant that brackets its way in) and was NOT flown here because it answers a different question and would mean editing away the shape that produced the finding. `icon-off-orbit` is deliberately NOT added to allowedAnomalies - that would budget away the only thing this lane found (V1-map-dwell-mun-orbit is the precedent for a lane standing red by finding). WHAT THE LANE ALSO MEASURED, all green: `factory chain rec=d1a91f6b idx=1 inst=0 phases=10 reaimed=False window=[212.2,277204.4] faithfulFallback=False`, `Created ghost vessel ... body=Minmus sma=-32531 ecc=4.026861 (from visible-segment) segmentUT=267741.3-273833.0`, `phase=GhostCreated surface=ProtoIcon ... body=Minmus scene=TRACKSTATION`, and `ParsekTrackingStation initialized: created 1 ghost vessel(s)` - i.e. the predicted Minmus-framed hyperbola DID materialize in the tracking station on a faithful chain, anti-vacuity count 1 | D14 minmus/scene-ts/sandbox. NOT claimed: D11 phase-lock (V6T claims it off the identical two-token gate on the identical code path), D11 zero-drift-schedule (V7M owns it), any D11 arrival cell, D6 ghost-map-presence, D9 fast-forward. REPORT-ONLY, and DELIBERATELY LEFT SO by the 2026-08-08 arming sweep that armed V6M/V6T/V7M: both save-structure blocks stay declared and UNGATED even though both flights measured every window met (rewindPoints 0, supersedeRows 0, tombstones 0, committedTrees 1, trees 1). The reason is the posture, not the data - arming a second gate on a lane whose verdict already carries a finding pins windows no GREEN run ever confirmed. Arm only once the lane can go green on its own terms **MAP-PROBE ANTI-VACUITY PIN ADDED + RE-FLOWN 2026-08-09 (`2026-08-09_0931`), AND THE LANE STAYED RED BY FINDING - which is the correct outcome, not a regression.** `PARSEK-FAIL(anomaly)` attempt 1, wall 52 s, `anomalySweep hits=['icon-off-orbit'] counts={'icon-off-orbit': 1}`, reproducing both 2026-08-08 flights exactly; the new pin neither caused it nor changes it.
